115 年 8 月海關進出口貿易初步統計 
一、進出口貿易變動 
（一）出口 
8 月出口 824.0 億美元，創歷年單月新高，較上月增 9.4%，較上年
同月增 41.0%(按新臺幣計算增 52.6%)；累計 1 至 8 月出口 5,743.4
億美元，較上年同期增 44.2%(按新臺幣計算增 46.5%)。 
（二）進口 
8 月進口 601.0 億美元，為歷年單月第 3 高，較上月增 3.4%，較上
年同月增 44.3%(按新臺幣計算增 56.2%)；累 計1 至 8 月進口 4,374.3
億美元，較上年同期增 40.4%(按新臺幣計算增 42.4%)。 
（三）出入超 
8 月出超 223.0 億美元，為單月新高，較上年同月增 55.2 億美元；
累計 1 至 8 月出超 1,369.1 億美元，較上年同期增 500.5 億美元。

### Page 2

2 
二、出口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
受惠於人工智慧(AI)、 高效能運算及雲端服務等應用持續擴增，相關
產品出貨維持強勁， 加上價格攀升效 應，8月出口較上年同月增 239.7
億美元(+41.0%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 電子零組件出口 322.2 億美元(歷年單月新高)， 較上年同月增118.3
億美元(+58.0%)，其中積體電路增 115.5 億美元(+60.0%)、印刷電
路增 1.4 億美元(+29.9%)、 電容器及電阻器增0.7 億美元(+24.9%)。 
2. 資通與視聽產品出口 331.0 億美元，較上年同月增 97.6 億美元
(+41.8%)，其中電腦及其附屬單元增68.7 億美元(+40.4%)、儲存媒
體增 19.1 億美元(+2.4 倍)、 交換器及路由器增9.7 億美元(+1.1 倍)。 
3. 礦產品出口 20.0 億美元，較上年同月增 8.3 億美元(+70.2%)，其
中石油煉製品增 7.8 億美元(+74.3%)。 
4. 基本金屬及其製品出口 29.6 億美元，較上年同月增 5.4 億美元
(+22.4%)，其中銅及其製品增 6.1 億美元(+1.5 倍)，鋼鐵及其製品
減 1.1 億美元(-8.7%)。 
5. 機械出口 26.8 億美元，較上年同月增 3.6 億美元(+15.2%)，其中
生產半導體等機械增 1.9 億美元(+42.1%)、未列名具特殊功能機械
增 0.4 億美元(+39.0%)。

（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 8 月出口較上年同期增 1,759.8 億美元(+44.2%)，以資通與
視聽產品增 1,023.7 億美元(+68.5%)、電子零組件增 629.5 億美元
(+45.8%)增加較多。

生產等出現結構性轉變，爰另予併計。

三、進口主要貨品 
（一）與上年同月比較 
由於 AI 產業鏈國際分工與出口衍生需求之帶動，積體電路及資通產
品進口維持強勁，加上輸入物價明顯上漲，8 月總進口較上年同月增
184.5 億美元(+44.3%)，主要變動如下： 
1. 農工原料進口408.4 億美元， 較上年同月增129.4億美元(+46.4%)，
其中電子零組件增 82.5 億美元(+62.6%)、礦產品增 16.9 億美元
(+38.4%)、電腦之零附件增 14.6 億美元(+84.4%)、基本金屬增 4.5
億美元(+21.8%)、塑化原料增 4.0 億美元(+16.2%)。 
2. 資本設備進口 130.6 億美元， 較上年同月增 38.4 億美元(+41.6%)，
其中資通設備增 27.2 億美元(+1.2 倍)、半導體設備增 2.8 億美元
(+7.3%)、交通運輸設備增 2.8 億美元(+1.1 倍)。 
3. 消費品進口 55.7 億美元，較上年同月增 16.0 億美元(+40.5%)，其
中固態非揮發性儲存裝置增12.1 億美元(+3.7 倍)、藥粧用品增 1.2
億美元(+17.4%)、手機增 1.1 億美元(+78.1%)。

四、主要出口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
8 月對陸港、日本出口創新高，對美國、東協為單月第 3 高 
1. 對美國出口 236.7 億美元，較上年同月增 40.6 億美元(+20.7%)，
其中資通與視聽產品增 23.0 億美元(+14.6%)、電子零組件增 11.6
億美元(+1.5 倍)、電機產品增 1.7 億美元(+30.1%)。 
2. 對中國大陸與香港出口 211.5 億美元，較上年同月增 59.7 億美元
(+39.3%)，其中電子零組件增 47.4 億美元(+45.6%)、資通與視聽
產品增 6.7 億美元(+42.9%)、 基本金屬及其製品增4.6 億美元(+1.2
倍)。 
3. 對東協出口 151.3 億美元，較上年同月增 44.9 億美元(+42.2%)，
其中電子零組件增 25.3 億美元(+50.3%)、資通與視聽產品增 12.6
億美元(+41.1%)、礦產品增 4.6 億美元(+1.5 倍)。 
4. 對歐洲出口 44.5 億美元，較上年同月增 11.5 億美元(+34.8%)，其
中資通與視聽產品增 6.6 億美元(+57.0%)、電子零組件增 4.0 億美
元(+1.1 倍)，基本金屬及其製品減 1.4 億美元(-31.0%)。 
5. 對日本出口 36.6 億美元，較上年同月增8.9 億美元(+32.0%)，其中
電子零組件增 8.9 億美元(+76.7%)、 化學品增0.5 億美元(+32.3%)，
資通與視聽產品減 0.6 億美元(-13.9%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 8 月對美國出口 1,803.2 億美元，較上年同期增 631.6 億美
元(+53.9%)，對中國大陸與香港出口 1,394.7 億美元，增 307.5 億美
元(+28.3%)， 對東協出口1,150.7 億美元 ， 增372.3 億美元(+47.8%)，
對歐洲出口 376.6 億美元 ， 增130.7 億美元(+53.1%)， 對日本出口247.0
億美元，增 54.7 億美元(+28.4%)。

五、主要進口國家/地區 
（一）與上年同月比較 
8 月自中國大陸與香港進口 126.8 億美元 ， 較上年同月增47.6 億美元
(+60.2%)，自東協進口 106.9 億美元，增46.7 億美元(+77.6%)，自南
韓進口 91.7 億美元，增29.6 億美元(+47.7%)，自日本進口 57.0 億美
元，增 11.2 億美元(+24.4%)，自美國進口 53.9 億美元，增 14.3 億美
元(+35.9%)，自歐洲進口 49.5 億美元，增 6.2 億美元(+14.3%)。 
（二）與上年同期比較 
累計 1 至 8 月自主要國家/地區進口與上年同期比較 ， 自東協增75.4%、
自南韓增 63.2%、自中國大陸與香港增 40.5%、自美國增 24.4%、自
日本增 24.1%、自歐洲增 12.8%。

六、主要國家/地區貿易概況 
（一）今年 1-7 月主要國家/地區外貿景氣普遍上升，南韓(1-8 月)、香港、
新加坡出口動能尤為強勁，分別年增 52.8%、46.6%、38.3%；中國
大陸(1-8 月)、美國出口分別年增 19.3%及 14.6%，德國(1-6 月)、日
本各年增 10.9%、8.1%。 
（二）主要國家/地區進口同步上揚，累計 1-7 月以香港進口年增 48.1%最
為顯著， 新加坡、 中國大陸(1-8 月)分別年增 37.0%、27.0%，南韓(1-8
月)、德國(1-6 月)各增 18.7%、11.7%，日本、美國各增 6.5%、1.2%。

圖 5 2026 年主要國家/地區出進口年增率
七、綜合分析
（一）得益於人工智慧(AI)、高效能運算及 雲端服務等應用 需求強勁， 加
以外銷產品價格攀升，8 月出口 824.0 億美元，創歷年單月新高，年
增 41.0%，連續 34 個月正成長；進口 601.0 億美元，年增 44.3%，
主因 AI 產業鏈國際分工與出口衍生需求擴張 ，及農工原料行情上
漲；出、進口互抵後，出超 223.0 億美元，亦創單月新高 。累計 1
至 8 月出口年增 44.2%、進口增 40.4%。
（二）8 月出口主要貨品以電子零組件、資通與視聽產品貢獻最多，年增率
分別為 58.0%及 41.8%，前者受惠 AI 商機及記憶體外銷價量齊揚，規
模值創單月新高，兩類併計增 49.3%，其餘貨類平均增 16.3%，以礦
產品隨油品售價上漲，年增 70.2%較為突出，基本金屬及其製品、電
機產品各增 22.4%、22.3%。累計 1 至 8 月資通與視聽產品出口值已
超越歷年水準，與電子零組件合計占總出口比重達 78.7%。

（三）8 月對主要市場出口同呈雙位數成長，其中對中國大陸與香港、日本
受電子零組件外銷強勁推升，出口值同創單月新高，各年增 39.3%、
32.0%，對東協、歐洲、美國分別增 42.2%、34.8%、20.7%；五大市
場之外，對墨西哥及澳大利亞出口皆增逾 2 倍。累計 1 至 8 月對美
國、歐洲出口各年增 53.9%、53.1%，對東協年增 47.8%，對日本、
陸港各增 28.4%、28.3%，對五大市場出口規模均為歷年同期最佳。
（四）展望未來，地緣政治衝突 及美國關稅政策變化等不確定 因素，持續
干擾全球經濟成長力道，惟隨人工智慧應用 不斷深化，算力需求攀
升，國際雲端服務業者加速建置 AI 基礎設施，加以國內半導體及伺
服器等供應鏈具競爭優勢，均有助支撐我國外銷動能，下半年出口
可望維持成長態勢。

Wiwynn (6669.TW): New ASIC and GPU AI server racks in expansion; 
Aug strong beat; TP up to NT$3,777; Buy
21 September 2026 | 6:21AM HKT
Wiwynn delivered strong Aug revenues, which were 27% ahead of our estimates, 
reflecting strong general servers demand from US leading CSPs, and its major 
customers’ new ASIC AI servers ramping up. We expect sequential revenues growth 
to continue through Dec 2026, leading 3Q26 / 4Q26 revenues to grow at +46% / 
+22% QoQ, driven by (1) major customers’ new ASIC AI servers ramping up, across 
air-cooled and liquid-cooled, (2) general servers end demand remaining solid on 
agentic AI, (3) new GPU-powered AI server racks entering mass production in 4Q26 
across NVIDIA and AMD platforms, and (4) potential new customers in ASIC AI 
servers. Maintain Buy with TP raised to NT$3,777 (21.5x 2027E P/E vs. +32% EPS YoY 
in 2027-28E).

Ongoing capacity expansion: As we highlighted in Aug (report link), Wiwynn 
announced a 2H26 capex budget of US$942m, implying 2026 capex could be up to 
NT$40bn (US$1.3bn). On Sep 16, the company announced equipment procurement 
of US$16m (NT$512m), reflecting the strong AI demand and the company’s 
commitments on capacity expansion. We estimate the company’s capex at NT$44bn 
in 2026E, and to stay at a similar high level through 2028E, supporting its production 
sites diversification across the US, Mexico, and Malaysia, AI servers expansion from 
ASIC to GPU-powered, and products expansion across different ASICs and GPUs, 
along with further customers penetration. 
Earnings revision: We factor in August revenues, and change our net income by +7% 
/ +5% / +3% in 2026-28E. The change is mainly on revenues: we raise 2026-28E 
revenues by 8% / 7% / 5%, reflecting the strong beat of Aug revenues driven by

### Page 2

growing AI demand, which is also driving its general servers growth. Our EPS are reduced 
as we update the share count, reflecting the company’s stock split that started from 
Sept 2026. The mathematical effect on our EPS is around 66%.

Valuation: We continue to derive our 12M TP based on near-term P/E, and our 2027E 
target P/E increases slightly to 21.5x (vs. 20x previously), which is still derived from the 
peers’ updated correlation of forward trading P/E and EPS growth. Accordingly, our 12M 
TP increases by 13% to NT$3,777.00 (vs. NT$3,352.55 previously). Our target P/E of 
21.5x is above the company’s historical avg.+1stv. forward trading P/E, reflecting our 
positive view on Wiwynn’s leading position in ASIC AI server ODM along with product 
expansion (from ASIC to GPU AI servers and general server) and customers expansion. 
Maintain Buy.

### Page 3

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Wiwynn 
We are Buy-rated on Wiwynn with a 12-month TP of NT$3,777. Our target price is 
based on 21.5x 2027E P/E, which is derived from PC/server peers’ EPS growth vs. P/E 
multiple correlation. We see EPS growth as a major factor for the stock’s performance. 
Key downside risks: 1) weaker-than-expected AI server demand growth; 2) 
slower-than-expected recovery in general server demand; and 3) more severe 
competition in the server ODM market.

Document 179: AI大神Karpathy最新洞察：未来我们更该“理解”大模型输出，而不是被它淹没

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI大神Karpathy最新洞察：未来我们更该“理解”大模型输出，而不是被它淹没.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-03
Pages: 4
Status: ok

### Page 1

⼤模型越来越强，⽣成内容越来越⻓、越来越复杂，⼈类反⽽容易陷⼊ “ 信息过载 ” 。  真正
的价值，不再是 “ 让它⽣成 ” ，⽽是 “ 如何⾼效、清晰地理解它⽣成的东西 ” 。
摘要：
最近， AI 领域的传奇⼈物 Andrej Karpathy （前 OpenAI 联合创始⼈、特斯拉 AI 总监）在 X 上发了
⼀条极具前瞻性的帖⼦，迅速引发⼴泛讨论。
他开⻔⻅⼭地说： “ 我们将花越来越多的时间去理解语⾔模型的输出。 ”
这句话看似简单，却精准点出了当前 AI 使⽤的核⼼痛点：⼤模型越来越强，⽣成内容越来越⻓、
越来越复杂，⼈类反⽽容易陷⼊ “ 信息过载 ” 。  真正的价值，不再是 “ 让它⽣成 ” ，⽽是 “ 如何⾼
效、清晰地理解它⽣成的东西 ” 。
Karpathy 分享了⼏个他亲⾃验证过的实⽤技巧，从⽂字到图表，再到交互⽹⻚和解释视频，层
层递进。今天我们就来了解⼀下这些⽅法，并补充相关背景知识，希望帮助⼤家在实际⼯作中
真正落地。
写作层⾯：⽤ ASD-STE100“ 控制语⾔ ” 逼出清晰表达

⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

Karpathy 最推荐的⽅法之⼀是：直接要求⼤模型⽤ ASD-STE100 来解释内容。
ASD-STE100 全称是 Simplified Technical English （简化技术英语），最初是欧洲航空航天⼯业
在上世纪 80 年代为⻜机维修⼿册开发的受控⾃然语⾔。⽬的很明确：就是让⾮英语⺟语的技术
⼈员也能准确弄懂关键安全信息，避免因歧义导致事故。
它的核⼼特点包括：
• 约 900 个批准词汇，每个词只有⼀个明确含义
• 严格的写作规则：主动语态、短句、⼀句话⼀个指令、避免被动语态和不必要的同义词
• 句⼦⻓度和段落结构都有明确限制
Karpathy 发现，⼤模型对这种规范⾮常熟悉，⽣成的⽂字往往简洁、⼲净、易读。如果觉得太
严格，可以稍微放松，⽐如要求 “ 达到 ASD-STE100 的 80% 程度 ” 。
实际体验下来，⽤这种⻛格解释复杂技术概念时，冗余⼤幅减少，逻辑更清晰，特别适合技术
⽂档、产品说明或内部知识沉淀。
更进⼀步：直接⽣成图表和图像
⽂字再清晰，有时也不如图表直观。

Karpathy 建议：别只让它写，直接让⼤模型⽣成图表或⽰意图。视觉信息往往⽐纯⽂字更容易
处理和记忆。⽆论是流程图、架构图还是概念对⽐图，⼤模型现在的视觉⽣成能⼒已经相当成
熟，能把抽象概念具象化。
再升级：输出成精美可交互的 HTML ⽹⻚
⽐图表更强的是完整⽹⻚。
只需在提⽰词⾥加上 “ ⽤ HTML 输出 ” ，⼤模型就能⽣成带样式、动画甚⾄简单交互的⻚⾯。前端
能⼒近年突⻜猛进，⽣成的⻚⾯往往美观且实⽤，适合⽤来做演⽰、知识库或临时⼯具。
这种⽅式直接把 “ 阅读 ” 变成了 “ 体验 ” ，理解成本进⼀步降低。
最看好的⽅向：定制化解释视频（ 3Blue1Brown ⻛格）
Karpathy 最看好的输出形式，是完全定制的解释视频。
他举例：可以要求 “ ⽤ 3Blue1Brown ⻛格制作关于 X 主题的解释视频，并⽤ ElevenLabs API 做语
⾳旁⽩ ” 。
3Blue1Brown （ Grant Sanderson ）以精美的数学动画闻名，其开源动画库 Manim 已被社区⼴泛

使⽤。现在⼤模型已经能较好地⽣成 Manim 代码，再结合语⾳合成，确实能够产出可⽤的教学
视频。
这可能代表⼀种范式转变：过去为理解⼀个复杂概念，我们需要花费⼤量时间阅读或等待专业
视频出现；现在已经可以直接按需⽣成 “ 专属解释视频 ” 。

### Page 3

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
158 收藏 分享到 :
写评论
从 “ 使⽤ AI” 到 “ 监督与理解 AI”
Karpathy 在帖⼦结尾点出了更深⼀层的趋势：随着⼤模型能⼒持续提升，它们会越来越多地⾃
主完成底层⼯作。⼈类的⼯作会向更⾼抽象层级移动，那就是监督、理解和把关。
幸运的是，⼤模型本⾝也能帮我们完成这⼀转变。因为智能和代码变得极其丰富，我们可以让
它⽣成那些过去 “ 不值得专⻔制作 ” 的⼤型、定制、可抛弃的软件产物（⽹⻚、视频、交互⼯具
等）。
⼤胆尝试这些更⾼维度的输出形式，往往会带来惊喜。
具体实践建议（可以直接复制使⽤）：
• “ ⽤ ASD-STE100 ⻛格解释 [ 主题 ] ，要求清晰、主动语态、短句。 ”
• “ 为 [ 主题 ] ⽣成⼀张清晰的架构图 / 流程图。 ”
• “ ⽤ HTML 创建⼀个美观的交互式⽹⻚，解释 [ 主题 ] 。 ”

• “ ⽤ 3Blue1Brown ⻛格制作关于 [ 主题 ] 的解释视频脚本，并给出 Manim 代码思路。 ”
AI 真正改变⼯作⽅式的关键，不在于它能⽣成多少内容，⽽在于我们能否⾼效理解它⽣成的内
容。 Karpathy 的这条帖⼦，给所有 AI 重度⽤⼾指明了⼀条清晰的路径。

Document 189: AMD acquires World Labs to expand its AI Hardware-Software Co-Design

Source PDF: all report pdf/AMD acquires World Labs to expand its AI Hardware-Software Co-Design.pdf
Report source: Substack Reports
Date: 2026-09-28
Pages: 5

### Page 1

From:    陸⾏之  Andrew Lu on global semis and techs <andrewhclu@substack.com>
Subject: AMD acquires World Labs to expand its AI Hardware-Software Co-Design
Date:    9 hours ago
AMD acquires World Labs to expand its AI Hardware-Software Co-Design

AMD acquires World Labs to
expand its AI Hardware-
Software Co-Design
AMD 併購 World Labs 的綜效及⾵險 - 尤其加強軟硬兼施的 AI 晶⽚ / 系
統設計及稀釋 / 商譽減損的⾵險
ANDREW LU ON GLOBAL SEMI/TECHS
SEP 28 ∙ PAID
READ IN APP

What’s new?
AMD 以約  82 億美元全股票交易（ All-Stock Transaction ）收購由 AI 教⺟李
⾶⾶聯合創⽴的空間智能初創公司  World Labs ，是其繼收購賽靈思（ Xilinx,
500 億美元）、 Silo AI （ 6.65 億美元）及  ZT Systems （ 49 億美元）後，在
AI 領域另⼀前沿戰略布局。
依據協議，李⾶⾶將出任  AMD 執⾏副總裁兼⾸席科學家（ EVP & Chief
Scientist ），直接向董事⻑兼執⾏⻑蘇姿丰（ Lisa Su ）匯報，核⼼團隊（包括
共同創辦⼈  Justin Johnson 與  Ben Mildenhall ）將組成  AMD 旗下的前沿
AI 研究機構。這項交易象徵  AMD 從純硬體晶⽚商向「全端  AI 平台（ Full-
Stack AI Platform ）」的戰略轉移。

### Page 2

Synergies
1. Hardware-Software Co-Design ）
過去  AMD 在  AI 戰場的最⼤痛點是「先定義晶⽚硬體，再由軟體團隊艱難適
配  ROCm 」，導致⽣態進度⻑年落後  NVIDIA CUDA 。
模型導向架構（ Model-driven Silicon ）： World Labs 專注於空間智
能（ Spatial Intelligence ）與⼤型世界模型（ Large World Models,
LWMs ），涉及⾼並發的  3D 體積重建（ NeRF 、 3D Gaussian
Splatting ）、物理碰撞模擬及時空連續性計算。
付費訂閱戶可分享我們觀察到的 3 個併購綜效， 4 個⾵
險，尤其是股權稀釋及商譽攤銷對股東的影響，尤其是
未來的觀察指標
Subscribed
反哺硬體規格：頂級模型團隊直接進駐，能讓  AMD 在規劃下⼀代
Instinct 架構（如  MI400 系列及後續  UDNA 架構）時，精準針對底層
張量核⼼、記憶體⼦系統（ HBM 頻寬配置與容量）、晶⽚間互連

（ Inﬁnity Fabric ）進⾏客製化最佳化，擺脫被動追趕的窘境。

### Page 3

Source: Andrew Lu on global semis/techs
2. Shifting focus to physical AI & embodied AI
當前以⽂字與  2D 圖像為核⼼的⽣成式  AI 正逐步成熟，下⼀階段的兵家必爭
之地是理解物理規律、三維環境模擬與機器⼈交互。
抗衡  NVIDIA Omniverse 與  Cosmos： NVIDIA 憑藉  Omniverse
數位孿⽣與  Cosmos 世界基礎模型，深度綁定⾃駕⾞、機器⼈與⼯業製
造業者。 AMD 過去在此領域近乎空⽩， World Labs 的加⼊補⿑了關鍵
資產，使  AMD 具備建構開放式物理世界模擬平台的基⽯。
3. Buying elite AI software engineers
⼈才磁吸效應：李⾶⾶在⼈⼯智慧與計算機視覺領域具備學術威望。由其
擔任⾸席科學家，能⼤幅扭轉  AMD 「⼯程實⼒強但缺乏  AI 頂級科學家
陣容」的市場認知，對後續吸納全球頂尖演算法研究員與跨界⼈才具備指

標性意義。
推動開源社群擴張： World Labs 團隊的⼊駐，能從演算法源頭優化
ROCm 開源軟體堆疊對前沿多模態架構的⽀援，降低開發者在  AMD 硬
體上訓練與微調複雜模型的阻⼒。
Risks and challenges
Source: Andrew Lu on global semis/techs
1. 財務溢價過⾼與商譽減損⾵險
World Labs 於  2024 年成⽴，早期融資估值約在  10 億美元左右，本次
AMD ⽀付  82 億美元，隱含極⾼的收購溢價。
儘管採全股票收購避免了現⾦儲備消耗，但會直接稀釋流通股東權益。
若未來  3 ⾄  5 年內該技術未能實質帶動  Instinct 晶⽚或系統出貨量激
增， AMD 將⾯臨商譽（ Goodwill ）與無形資產減損的提列壓⼒。我們⽬
前預測  AMD 未來 5 年每年將額外攤銷 15 億美元軟體資產溢價，讓年度折

### Page 4

舊及攤銷從 30 億美元增加 50% 到 45 億美元，除了 3-4% 股權稀釋外，影
響 0.9 美元年度 GAAP EPS 或 2027 年度彭博社分析師預期的  13.66 美元
GAAP EPS 的 6.6% ，但未來 5 年  Non-GAAP EPS 預測不受影響。
2. 研發燃燒率（ R&D Cash Burn ）與商業化空窗期
世界模型與物理模擬訓練需要極端龐⼤的算⼒資源與⼯程投⼊。空間智能⽬前
距離廣泛的企業級商業變現仍有⼀定距離，短期內  World Labs 不僅無法貢獻
⾃由現⾦流，還需佔⽤⼤量內部  GPU 叢集與研發預算，可能在短期內對
AMD 的⾮  GAAP 營運利潤率（ Operating Margin ）造成壓⼒。
3. 組織⽂化整合與頂尖⼈才留任
硬體半導體企業的⽂化核⼼是「嚴格的產品進度表、良率提升與供應鏈管
控」；⽽前沿  AI 實驗室的核⼼則是「開放式論⽂發表、⾃由度極⾼的算⼒
探索」。
過去⼤廠收購純研究型  AI 團隊後，常因官僚流程或研究⾃由受限導致核

⼼⼈才流失。李⾶⾶團隊能否在體量龐⼤的半導體公司架構內保持研究活
⼒，取決於管理層授予的獨⽴性與激勵機制設計。
4. 與下游客戶的競合衝突（ Channel Conﬂict ）
AMD 先前定位是純粹的中⽴算⼒供應商，⼒求承接微軟、 Meta 、 AWS
等不想被  NVIDIA 壟斷的轉單需求。
當  AMD ⾃⾝具備由李⾶⾶領軍的前沿模型團隊後，如何在「⾃研前沿模
型技術」與「⽀援客戶⾃研模型」之間維持中⽴界線，需謹慎拿捏，避免
讓雲端巨頭與下游  AI 新創產⽣戒⼼。
The points in future to check
後續評估此項併購成效的核⼼觀察點包含：
1. 硬體轉化速度： World Labs 的演算法研究能否在  12–18 個⽉內實質轉
化為  AMD UDNA/ROCm 上的專屬優化庫，並直接體現在  MI400 等下
⼀代晶⽚的基準測試（ Benchmarks ）上。
2. ⼈才留存率：李⾶⾶及其核⼼研究團隊在交易完成後的留任年限與論⽂ /
架構產出穩定度。

3. ⽣態聯盟建⽴： AMD 是否能以  World Labs 為核⼼，聯合⾃駕⾞、機器
⼈、遊戲引擎（如  Epic/Unity ）及⼯業製造巨頭，建⽴抗衡  NVIDIA
Omniverse 的開源標準體系。
這項交易本質上是  AMD 在完成晶⽚與系統（ ZT Systems ）拼圖後，向  AI
價值鏈最⾼端「認知架構與世界模型」邁出的防禦兼進攻型豪賭；若整合順
暢， AMD 將具備從底層矽智財到實體  AI 基礎模型的端到端定義能⼒，但其昂
貴的代價與漫⻑的技術轉化週期，將⾼度考驗管理層的整合與資本配置能⼒。

2 of 8 September 21, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
The Yen, 10-Year Yield and Iran:  Bessent’s Three-Front War and the Stakes for Investors
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Scott Bessent may carry the title of Treasury Secretary, but these days he looks more like
America’s financial field marshal. His battlefield stretches from Tokyo, where he is trying
to engineer a stronger Japan yen; to the U.S. Treasury market, where he is battling to keep

the 10-year yield from breaking decisively above 5%; and Tehran, where he has become the
point man in the escalating U.S. campaign to squeeze Iran financially and economically.
Make no mistake about it: Secretary Bessent and the Treasury Department are increasingly
an instrument of American economic statecraft, charged not simply with financing the
government and overseeing the financial system, but with managing markets, currencies
and geopolitical adversaries simultaneously. Welcome to the new age of financial warfare.

Front One: Defending the Yen. The first front unfolded over the summer when Secretary
Bessent and the BoJ stealthily intervened in support of the yen, which had fallen to a near
40-year low against the dollar by late July. To arrest the yen’s decline, the U.S. Treasury sold
euros instead of dollars in support of the yen, while pushing the BoJ to make use of the
Fed’s emergency repo facilities. In further support of the yen, the BoJ did what Bessent and

the markets have been pining for, raising its official rate by 25 basis points (bps) to 1.25%.
Since intervening in the foreign exchange markets, the yen has appreciated by nearly 5%
against the U.S. dollar, alleviating concerns of a disorderly unwind of the yen and giving
Bessent—thus far—an important victory. Defending the yen, of course, isn’t simply about
Japan—it’s about the U.S. and the need for foreign capital inflows.
Japan is one of America’s largest foreign creditors. A persistently weak yen creates

pressure on Tokyo to defend its currency, potentially by selling some of its enormous
holdings of foreign assets, including U.S. Treasurys. That prospect didn’t sit well with the
Treasury secretary. At precisely the moment Washington needs foreign capital to finance
its enormous fiscal deficits, the last thing Mr. Bessent wants is one of America’s largest
creditors to become a significant seller of Treasury securities. Hence, the battle to backstop

Japan’s currency.
Front Two: Defending the 10-Year Bond. Mr. Bessent has had less success on the
second front: the U.S. bond market. While the 10-year Treasury yield slipped slightly
following last week’s decision by the Fed to boost the fed funds rate by 25 bps, the
benchmark interest rate is up roughly 85 bps since the start of the year. Stubbornly high
inflation, elevated oil prices, enormous government borrowing requirements and growing
investor anxiety over America’s fiscal trajectory have all converged and conspired to keep

the 10-year yield closer to 5% than 4%.
Field Marshal Bessent is very aware that the 10-year Treasury is the gravitational center of
global finance—and that mortgage rates, corporate borrowing costs, equity valuations and
the government’s own interest bill all take their cue from it. A sustained move materially
above 5% would tighten financial conditions across the American economy, potentially
threaten the Equity bull market, and pile more pressure on the Republican Party.

To prevent this from happening, the Treasury has expanded its bond-buyback operations,
including purchases of longer-dated government securities, in an effort to improve liquidity
and relieve some pressure on the market, while shifting new government debt issuance
into short-term bills. Yet the bond vigilantes have so far proved harder to intimidate than
currency traders. Even after Treasury announced additional purchases, yields continued
climbing before receiving some relief from the latest hawkish move from the Fed.

Over the medium term, if investors believe Washington is borrowing too much, inflation is
running too hot or the supply of bonds is overwhelming global demand, they will demand a
higher yield to own America’s debt. This battle is far from over.
Front Three: Squeezing Tehran. Then there is Iran, where Mr. Bessent is wielding an
entirely different weapon: America’s control over the plumbing of global finance. In practice
Investment Implications
We expect continued investor

support and preference for real hard
assets, and a bias towards a plethora
of commodities and industrial plays.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 21, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
this means the Treasury can make a foreign bank choose between doing business
with Iran or the U.S. It can freeze assets, blacklist companies, restrict transactions and
pressure allies to close financial escape routes.
Under “Operation Economic Outcast,” billed as economic “D-Day,” Treasury has
intensified sanctions against Iranian entities and financial networks, including recent

actions against Iran aviation and additional efforts aimed at banks and organizations
facilitating Tehran’s international transactions. Mr. Bessent has described the objective
in unusually stark terms: to “economically asphyxiate” the Iran regime. This is economic
warfare in its purest form, but whether it works remains to be seen.
“Asphyxiating” an economy takes time and patience—and international cooperation
and coordination. It means closing multiple global loopholes of third-party entities still

willing and able to do business with Iran—look no further than Russian banks. In addition,
squeezing a major oil producer runs the risk of aggravating global oil supplies, keeping
oil prices elevated. Higher energy costs, in turn, feed right back into the U.S. inflation
outlook the Fed is trying to tame and keeps the bond vigilantes on guard and ready to
push back. Not unlike the battle to suppress U.S. bond yields, the battle to bring Iran to
heel is going to be a long one.

How does it all end? How Scott Bessent’s three-front war plays out remains to be seen. A
decisive victory on all three fronts is unlikely since corralling currency and bond markets
is next to impossible, while the whims of geopolitics can lay waste to the best-laid plans.
That said, however, the U.S. Treasury Secretary has signaled that he is ready to take
a very proactive, pro-growth approach to his job. That’s the good news. The less
encouraging news stems from the inconsistencies in the overarching objectives of the

Treasury secretary.
Mr. Bessent can pressure Iran because access to the dollar-based financial system
remains extraordinarily valuable. He can intervene in foreign-exchange markets because
America’s financial resources give Washington enormous clout. And he can attempt to
influence the Treasury market because U.S. government securities remain the foundation
of the global financial system. But therein lies the contradiction.
The U.S. is simultaneously weaponizing the financial system abroad while becoming more

dependent on that same financial system at home. Washington wants the world to fear
losing access to dollars while also asking the world to lend it trillions of dollars. It wants to
use Treasury and dollar dominance as instruments of geopolitical coercion while running
fiscal deficits that require foreigners to continue believing deeply in those very assets.
The more Washington borrows, the more sensitive it becomes to bond-market discipline.
The more aggressively it weaponizes access to the dollar, the greater the incentive for

adversaries to seek alternatives. And the more the Treasury secretary intervenes directly
in markets, the more investors may begin wondering where economic policy ends and
market management begins.

The Yield Rising 10-year Treasury yield Lower/contained yields Debt-management strategy, buybacks, issuance policy

For the moment, Mr. Bessent is fighting on three fronts. He wants a stronger yen, a
lower 10-year Treasury yield and an economically isolated Iran. Success on any one front
would be difficult. Achieving all three simultaneously will require considerable skill—and
perhaps a little luck.

MARKET VIEW
Beyond AI’s Resource Footprint, Consider its “Handprint”
Anna Potts, CFA, Vice President and Investment Strategist
Since the launch of ChatGPT, investors have celebrated AI’s potential while growing
more concerned about its resource footprint: Behind every AI-generated response is
a physical network of data centers, power plants, transmission lines, cooling systems,

semiconductor facilities and critical-mineral supply chains. No doubt, the AI revolution
has environmental costs. As adoption accelerates, growing demand for power, water,
advanced hardware and raw materials could strain grids, raise emissions and intensify
pressure on local resources. Yet the sustainability conversation should extend beyond
AI’s footprint to consider whether its environmental benefits can outweigh its
resource demands.
AI’s footprint is large, growing and certainly not trivial. AI increases electricity demand,

adds large, concentrated loads to already constrained grids, and may slow the retirement
of polluting energy assets. U.S. data centers could account for 20% of national electricity
consumption by 2035, up from 5.9% today, with power needs reaching 194 gigawatts
(comparable to the electricity demand of more than 150 million homes). 1 Cooling, power
generation and semiconductor manufacturing require water; each new megawatt also
embeds substantial copper, aluminum and other materials; and short hardware cycles

can increase electronic waste. Use varies sharply by workload: Complex reasoning and
agentic tasks can consume many times more energy than a simple text prompt. The
effects are already reaching consumers and communities. Wholesale power costs on
PJM—the largest U.S. grid—rose 76% year-over-year in Q1 2026, while data-center
load included in its previous two capacity auctions added an estimated $13.8 billion to
customer bills. 2 Communities are mobilizing around their concerns, including affordability,

water use and air pollution: Q1 2026 produced the largest single quarterly concentration
of delayed or blocked data-center projects on record. 3 Opposition is therefore becoming
a development constraint to be considered.
Context, caveats and comparisons. Headlines often tell only part of the story. Globally,
data centers account for about 1% of electricity use, 4 modest relative to many industries,
and 9% of estimated global 2025 to 2030 electricity growth. 5 A typical text prompt on

one major company’s AI model 6 uses 0.24 watt-hours of electricity, emits 0.03 grams
of CO₂ and consumes 0.26 milliliters of water—roughly 617,000 prompts match the
emissions from producing and cooking one sirloin steak, while 262,000 match the water
used in an eight-minute shower. 7 Still, billions of interactions, more complex workloads
and growth in resource-constrained regions can create meaningful cumulative impacts.
The most meaningful assessment therefore depends on workload, utilization, location,

grid mix, cooling design, local water availability and who bears the cost of supporting
infrastructure, not one universal number.
AI’s Handprint. While much remains to be seen, AI’s environmental “handprint”—the
benefits it can enable—could potentially prove larger than its direct footprint in some
areas. Across the energy system, AI can help utilities better match electricity supply
and demand, balance grids, optimize storage and reduce wasted energy. Data centers

already account for more than 30% of corporate renewable power-purchase agreements,
helping create long-dated demand for clean generation. 8 Beyond power, AI can improve
precision agriculture, streamline transportation and inventories, and support product

investment in power generation,

advanced cooling, water treatment
and reuse, efficient computing, and
materials recovery and recycling.
Diversified environmental and thematic
strategies may offer exposure to
these physical enablers while reducing
reliance on benchmark names.

design, predictive maintenance, waste sorting and recycling. AI is transforming climate
risk management by using satellite imagery, weather data and predictive analytics to
forecast floods, wildfires and extreme weather events, enabling governments, companies,
and investors to anticipate risks and allocate capital more effectively (Exhibit 2). These
gains are not automatic: they depend on innovation, adoption, and whether efficiency

improvements reduce total resource use rather than simply stimulate more demand. All
industries are faced with the opportunity to responsibly use AI to make operations more
efficient, and much is yet to be discovered.

AI’s potential handprint becomes more tangible in transportation, agriculture and climate
resilience. In aviation, for example, AI can combine weather forecasts, flight paths and
atmospheric conditions to reroute aircraft around regions where warming contrails
are most likely to form—reducing climate impact without requiring a new aircraft or

fuel system. On farms, machine learning can interpret aerial imagery and field-level
sensor data to identify crop stress early, directing water, fertilizer and chemicals to
where they are needed and helping growers improve yields while reducing inputs. AI
can also strengthen climate preparedness by analyzing satellite imagery, weather data
and infrastructure exposure to detect deforestation, anticipate floods or wildfires,
and identify communities, facilities and supply chains most vulnerable to physical

hazards. These are just a few examples that illustrate how AI’s value can extend beyond
incremental efficiency gains to reshape real-world decisions.
Investment Considerations. The challenges created by AI also create potential
investment opportunities. Companies are investing in efficient chips, closed-loop cooling,
reclaimed-water systems, energy storage, grid upgrades and cleaner firm power to
reduce resource intensity and improve reliability. Professionally managed environmental

and thematic strategies can target these “picks and shovels” of the digital buildout,
alongside businesses using AI to improve efficiency across energy management,
water infrastructure, environmental services, waste management and compute. A
disciplined thematic approach can diversify exposure beyond the largest benchmark
constituents while positioning for structural growth in power demand, grid modernization
and resource efficiency. Focusing solely on AI’s footprint risks overlooking both the

companies helping it scale more efficiently and the broader environmental benefits its
handprint may enable.

THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
The Beauty of September
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Call it an eventful first 13 trading days of September: Brent crude re-eclipsed $100 per
barrel, diesel prices surpassed $6 per gallon for the first time in history, the 10-year U.S.
Treasury yield breached 5%, 9 and the Fed joined the ECB and the BoJ in hiking rates for

the first time since July 2023. Through it all, the S&P 500 has shed 1.4% since the start
of the month, while its equal-weighted counterpart is down by 3.3%.
It’s not all too unexpected that September would be a choppy month for U.S. Equities.
Historical seasonal patterns suggest as much. Since 1928, the average S&P 500 return
in September has come out to -1.1%, 10 not only the worst month of the year on average
but also the only month when the benchmark has ended lower more often than it’s

ended higher. Similar weakness has been seen in midterm election years, with the S&P
500 producing an average September return of -0.8% since 1950. 10
The good news is that this has historically been an attractive entry point for U.S. Equity
investors. Call it the beauty of September: In contrast to September’s average decline,
average returns improve in October (+3.0%), November (+2.7%), and December (+0.8%).
Indeed, the S&P 500 has never been negative in the six or 12 months post-midterm

election since 1950 (Exhibit 4A), boasting an impressive average return of 13.8% and
15.8% over those respective periods. 10
Looking ahead to the coming weeks, we have little reason to believe that 2026
will diverge from the historical trend. Volatility is likely to rise into October before
moderating post-election, in line with what’s typical in the months surrounding the
midterms (Exhibit 4B). A quieter corporate calendar ahead of Q3 earnings means

investor focus will remain on incoming macro data, the path of energy prices, and the
Fed’s policy path.
In the meantime, while the S&P 500 still stands just 2.7% below its mid-August high,
churn beneath the surface already appears to be taking place. Just 38% of S&P 500
members now stand above their 50-day moving average versus 70% a mere month ago. 9
Far from worrisome, a correction in the near term in the context of a strong fundamental
economic backdrop would be a potential buying opportunity in our view.

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

Charts of the week: Extremely bearish bond positions

Editorial 
Inching towards a Fed ‘credibility’ hike 
Strategy summary:  August’s strong US jobs and producer 
inflation reports mean the Fed is most likely to hike next week 
or risk its credibility. However, a rate rise is unlikely to be the 
start of a hiking cycle. While higher oil prices fuel near -term 
inflation, driving bond yields to multi-year highs, we see inflation 
easing next year as the impact of tariffs and oil prices fades.  
There remains a residual risk that the Fed could be reluctant to 
hike rates next week, possibly due to pressure from the Trump 
administration. Any such hesitancy could lead to a short -term 
sell-off in equities and bonds as investors increasingly question 
the Fed’s independence. We would use any market volatility to 
add equities, given the robust earnings outlook, and increase 
bond maturities to 3-7 years. Regardless of the Fed decision, 
we are adding semiconductor equities as an opportunistic idea.  
A resilient job market. Last week, we said the chance of a Fed 
rate hike has risen above 50% after Chair Warsh’s hawkish 
Jackson Hole speech. The two remaining requirements that 
would secure a hike would be: i) a robust jobs report for August, 
followed by ii) an inflation report  showing core inflation at or 
above 0.2% m/m. The first requirement has been met – the US 
economy added 162,000 jobs in August, almost triple 
consensus estimates, with prior two months revised higher.  
Although around 60% of the job gains were in only two sectors 
– leisure & hospitality and government – and average hourly 
earnings continued its slide to a five-year low of 3.1% y/y, the 
crucial unemployment rate remained steady at 4.1% as labour 
force participation rate rose. The unemployment rate sugges ts 
the US economy is running at full employment. Meanwhile, US 
headline and core producer inflation accelerated in August. 
Inching towards a Fed ‘credibility’ hike:  A 25bps Fed hike 
next week now looks most likely. The only caveat to this outlook 
would be an underwhelmingly soft inflation report for August 
due tonight. With Fed Chair Warsh already characterising the 
current US policy rate of 3.75% as not restrictive, any failure to 
hike, due to pressure from the administration, would hurt the 
Fed’s credibility, impacting equities and bonds. The US 10-year 
yield surged this week 19bps to a three-year high of 4.97%.  
Extremely bearish positioning to limit bond yield upside:  
The US 10-year bond yield faces a major resistance level – 
2023 high of 5.02%. Moreover, investor positioning is extremely 
bearish (CTA short positions at record). Also, current yields are 
uncomfortably close to the “Trump put” levels. Authorities have 
several regulatory and bond supply and liquidity management 
tools to cap yields, at least in the near term. This implies limited 
scope for further upside in medium-to-long-term yields.  
Key risks: i) if the Fed holds next week, we see further near -
term sell-off on doubts about the Fed's independence, with the 
10-year yield  breaking above 5.02% briefly. ii) further oil  
spike. However, extreme positioning should still limit any bond 
yield upside. As such, we see the yield rise as an opportunity 
to raise maturity of bond allocations to 3- 7 years, 
preferring corporate bonds.  Longer-maturity bonds remain 
vulnerable to oil-driven inflation expectation and fiscal risks.  
Adding one more ECB rate hike after hawkish outlook: The 
ECB raised its deposit rate 25bps to 2.5%, as expected, and 
upgraded its growth and inflation forecasts for the coming 
years, citing a resilient economy and higher energy prices . 
President Lagarde was decidedly hawkish, while declining to 
commit to another hike.  The upgraded inflation forecasts, 
Lagarde’s comments and recent surge in gas prices lead us to 
add one more 25bps rate hike by year end (see page 8). 
Tactically adding semiconductor equities; using volatility 
to buy on dips:  This week’s softness in equity markets  was 
mainly driven higher oil prices and bond yields. We would use 
any volatility to add equities, especially in the US and Asia-ex -
Japan, where the corporate earnings outlook remains robust. In 
fact, we are using the weakness in semiconductor equities to 
add tactical exposure (see page 4 for more details). 
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 11 September 2026

Top client questions 
  Do you see any  opportunities for investors as rising 
memory chip prices are passed on to consumers? 
Our view: W ithin technology, we prefer semiconductors over 
hardware. We are initiating an Opportunistic idea on global 
semiconductors on the back of sustained AI infrastructure 
spend and attractive valuations. 
Rationale: Rising memory costs have contributed to higher PC and 
smartphone prices, yet hardware manufacturers have been unable 
to fully pass these costs on to consumers, resulting in continued 
margin pressure . In contrast, memory fundamentals remain 
supported by tight supply conditions and long-term agreements. That 
said, memory remains inherently cyclical and volatile, and investors 
should right-size their exposure to the industry appropriately.  
Looking more broadly at semiconductors, earnings growth continues 
to be supported by ongoing AI infrastructure spend and broadening 
AI adoption, with US AI adoption expected to reach 25.1% in Q4. 
The r esults season reaffirmed strong demand trends for 
semiconductors, while guidance points to persistent tightness in 
supply. The industry trades at around 16x forward earnings, below 
its five -year average of around 20x , despite the  strong growth 
outlook. We view the pullback in valuations as an attractive entry 
point and initiate an Opportunistic idea on global semiconductors.  
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  Does debt -funded AI capex pose a downgrade risk for 
hyperscalers? Does it create a material overhang for Developed 
Market (DM) investment-grade (IG) corporate bonds? 
Our view: Rating agencies do not assess credit quality on 
leverage metrics alone; they  look through the business cycle . 
DM IG corporate bond spreads could disperse across sectors.  
Rationale: The picture is mixed across hyperscalers, but the trend 
uniformly points to tighter credit headroom. Rating agencies do not 
assess credit quality on leverage metrics alone. Rather, they look 
through the business cycle, weighing capex intensity  against 
earnings trajectory and demand durability over a multi-year horizon. 
Currently, leading rating agencies expect hyperscaler leverage to 
peak around 2027, followed by deleveraging as capex subsides and 
earnings continue growing. That said, the downgrade risk could 
rise if deleveraging is delayed , demand weakens or idiosyncratic 
issues emerge, such as the use of unconventional, off-balance-sheet 
circular financing structures that obscure true leverage. 
For DM IG corporate bonds, the bias is broadly negative, driven by 
supply indigestion from heavy AI-related issuance, downgrade risk 
and index concentration distortion as a few large issuers dominate 
benchmark weightings. However, we do not expect a uniform sell-
off. Spreads could disperse across sectors , with AI capex-heavy 
sectors most likely to stay structurally wide. 
—  Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 11 September 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Will long-dated  US government bond yields  rise further 
following the US Treasury’s first expanded bond buyback? 
Our view: The bond buyback should flatten the US yield curve, 
but is unlikely to mark a structural shift. Given higher yields, we 
cautiously extend duration and favour the 3-7-year segment. 
Rationale: The US Treasury said it will purchase up to USD 6bn of 
longer-dated government debt  in the first operation under an 
expanded buyback programme.  However, bond markets remain  
underwhelmed, with investors viewing the scale of repurchases as 
insufficient relative to broader market expectations  and the sheer 
volume of outstanding long-term debt. As a result, y ields have 
continued their upward trajectory, with the US 10-year yield hitting 
a three-year high near 4.96% and the 30-year yield rising to 5.37%, 
suggesting markets are not yet convinced that the buyback  will 
materially shift the supply-demand balance for long-duration debt. 
Mechanically, funding long-end purchases via increased T reasury-
bill issuance introduces a flattening impulse to the yield curve. Still, 
we view this as a liquidity management tool rather than a 
structural pivot. We, therefore, do not expect it to be the dominant 
catalyst for a significant or sustained decline in long-term yields. 
Compounding matters, crude oil prices breaching USD 100/bbl will 
revive inflation concerns , likely  reinforcing a ‘higher-for-longer’ 
policy stance and offsetting the buyback's marginal relief. Against, 
this backdrop, w e advocate maintaining a defensive duration 
posture. Given higher yields, we cautiously extend duration  and 
favour the 3-7 year maturity bucket as inflation risks and heavy debt 
supply are keeping upward pressure on long-term yields near term. 
—  Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Where is the RBA headed on interest rates, and what does 
it mean for the AUD and AUD bonds? 
Our view: We see a rising probability of a September RBA rate  
hike, but a second hike in November is not our base case. 
Rationale: The RBA remains focused on inflation, having already 
hiked its policy rate three times in 2026 – the most of any G7 central 
bank. Beyond a stronger -than-expected July CPI print, strong 
business investment and a tight labour market  continue to reinforce 
the case for further tightening. A further hike is now more likely than 
we previously expected, but back-to-back hikes is not our base case.  
Meanwhile, AUD/USD has risen to the 0.72 level, and the Australia 
10-year government bond yield has climbed over 70bps over the 
past six months, with the move accelerat ing sharply since late 
August as rate-hike expectations surged. We expect both the AUD 
and bond yields to remain within their recent range, as the hawkish 
policy shift has largely already been priced in by markets. 
—  Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 11 September 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  How does the JPY appreciation affect your view on Japan 
equities and key sectors? 
Our view: Japan equities remain a Core holding, with Japan 
financials as our preferred sector. 
Rationale: The JPY  appreciation sharpens the earnings 
divergence between domestically oriented sectors and exporters, 
given the significant share of overseas revenues across corporate 
Japan. Our preferred financial sector generates c.60% of its 
revenue domestically and stands to benefit from ongoing BoJ policy 
normalisation through wider net interest margins. Other domestic-
demand sectors, including services and construction, face limited 
currency-translation headwinds and should benefit from stronger 
wage growth and improved cost pass -through. Within technology, 
the semiconductor supply chain remains attractive, given Japan's 
strong global market position  and pricing power amid structural AI 
demand, offsetting the sector's c.78% overseas revenue exposure. 
Conversely, healthcare has limited domestic offsets to absorb the 
JPY strength, while it has the highest overseas revenue exposure at 
c.80%. Similarly, autos  are vulnerable, facing headwinds from 
currency appreciation, tariff pressures and intensifying competition. 
—  Jason Wong, Senior Equity Analyst 
  Is there a risk of further oil price rise? How do we hedge it? 
Our view: We forecast WTI oil at USD 90/bbl in three months, 
but risks are skewed to the upside.  US Treasury Inflation -
protected Securities (TIPS) remain our preferred hedge. 
Rationale: Three developments would lead us to revise our 
forecast higher, with the first two already in motion: (1) A prolonged 
disruption to Gulf exports that accelerates global oil inventory 
draws. US crude oil stocks are visibly thinner,  trending below their 
five-year range. 
(2) Lasting damage to production or export infrastructure.  
Recent strikes on Saudi energy assets raise concerns more about 
the resilience of alternative export routes than about immediate 
production losses. Tighter US action against Iran- linked tankers 
could further constrain flows. (3) Stronger-than-expected demand 
that accelerates the tightening of physical balances. China appears 
to have resumed buying. Conversely, a credible normalisation in the 
Middle East remains the largest downside risk. With gasoline around 
USD 5 per gallon, the US administration has a clear incentive to 
facilitate a normalisation before the November US midterm elections. 
We prefer to hedge upside oil risk at the portfolio level rather than 
through outright oil -sensitive exposure, with US TIPS being our 
preferred route against higher headline inflation. 
—  Anthony Naab, CFA, Investment Strategist

### Page 7

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 11 September 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What is your outlook for the EUR, GBP and JPY against 
the backdrop of this month’s central bank policy meetings? 
Our view:  EUR/USD is supported by the ECB's  monetary 
tightening path, but an increasingly likely Fed hike could limit 
its near-term upside. The JPY is likely  relatively strong as w e 
expect the BoJ to catch up with rate hikes. Meanwhile, the GBP 
remains vulnerable amid  uncertainty around  the UK’s 
upcoming Autumn Budget.  
Rationale: The ECB hiked rates by 25bps to 2.5% as expected , 
with the market now pricing in another hike in December. Euro area 
annual consumer inflation rose to 3.3% in August  – the highest 
in nearly three years  – driven by energy pass -through from the 
Middle East conflict. The ECB also raised its 2027 growth forecast 
to 1.4% from 1.2%, headline inflation forecast to 2.5% from 2.3% and 
core inflation forecast to 2.6% from 2.5%. Greater economic 
resilience and persistent underlying inflation leave the ECB open 
to another hike.  However, wage growth moderated to 3.3% in Q2 
(from 3.5%), approaching the ECB's c.3% compatibility threshold, 
which could temper the pace of subsequent hikes. We see gradual 
upside risk for EUR/USD, with resistance at 1.18 in the coming 
weeks, although a Fed hike could delay a sustained break higher. 
We now  see a Fed hike as increasingly likely next week , 
especially if core consumer price inflation rises by at least 0.2% m/m. 
August producer price inflation came in higher than expected. Firm 
WTI oil prices above USD 100/bbl increase the risk of higher 
inflation and inflation expectations  in the months ahead. A hike, or 
guidance signalling further tightening, would support the USD, 
limiting the extent of EUR, JPY and GBP gains against it. We expect 
the USD Index (DXY) to be capped near 99.8. 
The USD/JPY repricing reinforces our concern about  BoJ policy 
normalisation. Downside risks towards 150-152 remain significant, 
but a Fed hike and  still-large US -Japan yield and growth 
differentials should provide some support. A cautious BoJ message 
could trigger a rebound, particularly after the recent sharp 
adjustment. We expect one hike next week, followed by another  in 
Q4 (likely in December) and potentially one each in Q1 and Q2 2027. 
BoE Governor Bailey has  previously resisted calls for immediate 
tightening, preferring greater clarity on second- round inflation 
effects. However, delayed energy price pass-through  and rising 
food prices are likely to push UK inflation  higher. While renewed 
rate hike expectations provide a structural underpinning for the GBP, 
the BoE's cautious stance relative to the ECB  and the UK 
Autumn Budget uncertainty  remain significant headwinds. We 
expect GBP/USD to remain rangebound, with a bearish bias near 
1.34. Above all, we see some upside potential in EUR/GBP.  
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist  
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Does the latest ECB meeting change your outlook on 
policy rates? 
Our view: The ECB raised its deposit rate by 25bps for the 
second time this year, taking it to 2.5%, in line with our 
expectations coming into this meeting. Given the new ECB staff 
projections that show higher inflation and stronger growth over 
the next couple of years, as well as ECB President Lagarde’s 
hawkish remarks at the press conference,  we are now 
projecting another deposit rate hike to 2.75% before year end.  
Rationale: The ECB cited the ongoing Middle East conflict as the 
primary driver of renewed inflationary pressure, with energy price 
shocks feeding through to broader price levels. The central bank 
stated that inflation will likely remain above 2%  for an "extended 
period," and updated staff projections revised inflation higher for 
2027 and 2028, with the 2028 inflation forecast now at 2.1%, slightly 
above the ECB's 2% target. 
ECB President Lagarde described this week’s hike as a "no brainer," 
with the decision bolstered by stronger inflation and growth 
projections. Key points from the press conference include: 
• The ECB is not pre- committing to a particular rate path , 
reiterating a meeting-by-meeting, data-dependent approach.  
• Lagarde noted that the Governing Council did not discuss 
future meetings , though the upward revision to the inflation and 
growth outlook has shifted the balance towards further tightening.  
• The hawks within the Governing Council appear to be 
winning the internal debate and would prefer to continue hiking, with 
the deposit rate potentially rising to at least 2.75%. 
• The Euro area economy's surprising resilience  was 
acknowledged as a factor supporting the decision.  
• Core inflation remains above 2%  through the end of the 
forecast horizon, a signal that the ECB does not view the current 
tightening cycle as complete. 
“Framework” guidance and market implications:  The hawkish 
shift in the  ECB’s updated projections and Lagarde’s decidedly 
hawkish comments lead us to expect another 25bps hike by 
December. The central bank's "meeting-by-meeting" stance means 
future decisions will be heavily influenced by incoming energy price 
data, second- round inflation effects and labour market 
developments. With seemingly no peace solution likely on the 
horizon in the Middle East, risks could continue to skew to the 
upside for policy rates as we head into 2027.  
—  Jonathan Liang, CFA, CIO for Fixed Income and FX

PROPRIETARY RESEARCH FROM LSEG September 4, 2026
WEEKLY AGGREGATES
ITEM DEFINITIONS AND METHODOLOGY
3© 2026 LSEG.  All marks herein are used under licence. TRPR_82222_867
General:
All aggregates are bottom-up aggregates calculated from analysts' consensus forecasts unless otherwise noted. Bottom-up
aggregates are weighted calculations using the I/B/E/S data from LSEG share class monitored or the official index weight of
the index-provider. Top-down forecasts are compilations of strategists' estimates. I/B/E/S universe calculations are based on

all companies within a given market monitored by LSEG.
Benchmark index calculations, including those for official local market indices, the S&P 500 Index, and the FTSE World Equity
Indices are calculated using official index constituents and weightings. All index composites are representative of current
index constituents. Index constituents and weightings are updated monthly during options expiration (the Thursday before the
third Friday of every month) for every index except the S&P 500, the constituents of which are current as of the date of

calculation.
PEIGBY refers to the Price Earnings Inverse Government Bond Yield ratio, which measures equity valuation with respect to
the inverse-yield on long-term government bonds (now known as the 'Fed' model). All PEIGBY ratios are I/B/E/S universe-
specific except for the US ratio, where it is based on the S&P 500 Index.
Methodology:
For US and selected Canadian aggregates, calendar-year aggregation is performed using the sum of four quarters' estimates

and a quarterly pro-ration is applied to non-December year end companies' quarterly EPS estimates. Outside of the US and
Canada, all company-level data are calendarized prior to aggregation using the Compustat method of calendarization.
Aggregation is performed using the annual consensus EPS estimate and a May roll-back is applied to December year-end
markets. Aggregates for Japan, where most companies report in March, use an August rollback and a November rollback is

used for Australia and New Zealand, where the fiscal year-end for most companies is in June.
All 12-month-forward data for ex-US markets are calculated using a pro-rata weighting to annual EPS forecasts. The
weightings applied are a daily calibration out of 365 days relative to each constituent's individual fiscal year-end and the
current date.
All non-surprise data, including aggregate forecast earnings, forecast growth, and forward-looking valuation calculations are

weighted according to the I/B/E/S share class monitored for I/B/E/S universes or the weightings assigned to each constituent
in a given index. Aggregated earnings calculations are the equivalent of net profit aggregates, as they are derived from the
EPS forecasts aggregated to the net income level by the appropriate share weighting.
Earnings Aggregation:
For 12-month-forward earnings, the 12-month-forward EPS is used in place of the calendarized EPS in the formulas below.

I/B/E/S universe EPS Aggregate = Index EPS Aggregate =
Growth Calculations:
All growth calculations are the weighted year over year growth in earnings for the calendarized fiscal year period. The
formulas below use index shares for index growth calculations.
Calendarized Earnings Growth = 12-month-forward Growth =
Valuation:
All valuation calculations are the weighted-average price-earnings ratio for the fiscal period in question. The formulas below

use index shares for index P/E calculations.
Calendarized P/E Aggregate = 12-month-forward P/E =
Revisions Momentum:
Unless otherwise indicated, analysts' revisions momentum is a simple aggregation of the total number of estimates raised
divided by the total number of estimates lowered. Thus, any revisions ratio less than 1 indicates negative revisions
momentum while a ratio greater than 1 indicates positive revisions momentum, with 1 marking parity.

The Global Aggregates from LSEG product contains annual aggregates for universes included in this publication, including
supplemental aggregate data of over 100 items as well as history dating back to 1985 for North America and to 1987 for other
markets. To learn more about this product, contact your LSEG sales representative.
FTSE and the FTSE World Equity Indices are owned by FTSE International Limited.

Document 289: Meta Muse爆红后遇留存瓶颈：打开率低于主流应用，长期变现面临考验

Source PDF: all report pdf/Meta Muse爆红后遇留存瓶颈：打开率低于主流应用，长期变现面临考验.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-04
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

Meta 旗下 AI 智能体 Muse 上线以来累计下载超 560 万次，⼀度登顶苹果应⽤商店，但⽤⼾打
开率仍低于⾕歌、 ChatGPT 等主流应⽤，留存与使⽤频率尚未跟上。法国巴黎银⾏据此下
调 Muse ⻓期变现预期，认为其短期难撼电商、旅游等垂直平台，未来商业化仍需依靠⽤⼾
参与度提升及⼴告等模式验证。
摘要：
Meta Platforms 旗下 AI 智能体 Muse 上线以来下载势头强劲，但⽤⼾留存与变现能⼒的双重瓶颈
正引发市场关注。法国巴黎银⾏对其⻓期货币化路径持审慎态度，揭⽰出消费级 AI 应⽤从获客到
留存这⼀普遍性转化难题。
据法国巴黎银⾏援引第三⽅数据， Muse ⾃ 9 ⽉推出以来累计下载量已超过 560 万次，⼀度登顶苹
果应⽤商店排⾏榜，上线初期在美国市场的下载表现甚⾄超过 ChatGPT 和 Sora 。然⽽，该⾏分
析师 Nick Jones 指出， Muse 的应⽤打开率⽬前虽已趋于稳定，但仍低于⾕歌、 ChatGPT 、
Instagram 、 WhatsApp 及 Facebook 等成熟应⽤，表明产品尚未融⼊⽤⼾⽇常使⽤习惯。

法国巴黎银⾏维持对 Meta 的 " 跑赢⼤盘 " 评级，⽬标价 885 美元，但同时下调了对 Muse ⻓期变现
能⼒的预期。 Jones 强调， Muse 能否将初期热度转化为持续活跃度，以及如何构建可持续的商
业模式，仍是投资者需要重点关注的核⼼问题。与此同时， OpenAI 本周推出消费级 AI 智能体新
产品 Dots ，消费级 AI 市场竞争格局正在加速演变。
社交⽣态助推下载，但打开率揭⽰留存瓶颈
Muse 上线初期的下载数据亮眼，在很⼤程度上得益于 Meta 庞⼤的社交平台⽣态。
Jones 表⽰，与需要从零建⽴⽤⼾基础的独⽴ AI 应⽤相⽐， Meta 可借助 Instagram 、 WhatsApp
和 Facebook 的现有⽤⼾覆盖，为 Muse 提供天然的推⼴渠道，同时显著压低⽤⼾获取成本。这⼀
分发优势使 Muse 在美国市场上线初期的下载量超过了 ChatGPT 和 Sora 。
值得注意的是， OpenAI 旗下视频⽣成应⽤ Sora 曾在推出后迅速⾛红，但此后已被整合进

ChatGPT ，不再作为独⽴应⽤运营 —— 这⼀先例提⽰市场，初期下载热度与产品⻓期⽣命⼒之
间存在明显落差。
对于 Muse ⽽⾔，下载量的快速积累并未同步转化为⾼频使⽤。
Jones 指出，Muse 打开率低于多款主流应⽤的现状，意味着产品⽬前仍处于培养⽤⼾使⽤习惯
的早期阶段，距离成为消费者⽇常频繁依赖的⼯具仍有距离。下⼀阶段的关键，将从推动下载
量增⻓转向提升⽤⼾留存率、使⽤频率与⻓期参与度。
短期难撼垂直平台，功能完整性是核⼼差距
随着 AI 智能体逐步具备信息搜索、产品⽐较和执⾏交易等能⼒，外界开始讨论此类产品是否会对
电商、旅游等垂直服务平台构成竞争威胁。法国巴黎银⾏对此持审慎⽴场。
Jones 认为，考虑到 Muse 当前⽤⼾普及程度有限，且缺乏垂直平台所具备的直接商品或服务供
给、完整交易体验以及客⼾服务能⼒，短期内难以对上述平台构成实质性冲击。专业电商或旅
游平台通常不仅提供信息检索与推荐，还拥有商品库存、交易处理、订单管理及售后⽀持等完
整体系 —— 即便 AI 智能体能够帮助⽤⼾发现产品，也未必能够独⽴完成整个交易闭环。

写评论
图⽚
Muse 未来能否改变消费者的购物与服务使⽤习惯，仍取决于 Meta 能否持续完善相关功能，并扩
⼤与外部商家的合作深度。
变现路径模糊，⼴告或成⻓期突破⼝
商业化路径是法国巴黎银⾏对 Muse 前景保持审慎的另⼀核⼼原因。
Jones 分析指出， Muse 现阶段主要通过向合作伙伴收取少量交易费⽤实现部分变现，同时采⽤
免费基础服务与付费订阅相结合的模式 —— 基础版本免费开放，⾼需求⽤⼾可按⽉付费获取更
⾼使⽤额度。
这⼀定价策略有助于 Meta 在产品发展初期扩⼤⽤⼾规模、提升参与度，⽽⾮过早追求收⼊最⼤
化。但 Jones 强调， Muse 的⻓期变现能⼒仍是投资者⾯临的重要不确定性。
法国巴黎银⾏认为，凭借 Meta 在数字⼴告领域积累的深厚经验，随着 Muse ⽤⼾参与度持续提

升，⼴告未来可能成为具有吸引⼒的商业化⽅向，使收⼊来源从订阅费和交易佣⾦逐步向⼴告
业务延伸。然⽽，在⽤⼾使⽤习惯尚未完全建⽴的当下，上述商业模式能否形成可观收⼊，仍
有待市场验证。
OpenAI Dots ⼊场，消费级 AI 竞争多维升级
消费级 AI 智能体市场的竞争格局正在加速演变。 Jones 特别关注 OpenAI 本周推出的新产品
Dots ，认为其未来能够获得多⼤程度的⽤⼾认可，将是观察这⼀市场竞争⾛向的重要指标。与
此同时，市场也在关注 Alphabet 等科技企业是否会推出具有竞争⼒的同类产品。
法国巴黎银⾏判断，随着越来越多企业探索利⽤ AI 完成复杂任务，消费级 AI 智能体市场的竞争将
逐渐从模型性能扩展⾄产品体验、⽤⼾留存、服务整合和商业化能⼒等多个维度。
对于 Meta ⽽⾔， Instagram 、 WhatsApp 和 Facebook 构成了重要的⽤⼾分发优势，但能否将这⼀
先天优势持续转化为 Muse 的使⽤需求增⻓，仍需要更多时间与数据加以检验。

SUMMARY: AI euphoria meets macro headwinds
Tech remained a major driver of equity market performance in Q3. The
Mag 7 outperformed the broader market while the S&P 500 also

posted gains, lifted by mega-cap and tech stocks.
Still, tech’s path in Q3 was volatile. July began with a reversal of the AI
trade as investors questioned elevated valuations, the sustainability of
AI spending and the outlook for semiconductor demand. That led to a
large sell-off in chip stocks, with large falls in Q2 outperformers SK
Hynix and Samsung contributing to a sharp decline in Korea’s KOSPI.
The Philly SOX experienced its worst monthly performance since

2008.
By August, however, sentiment had improved. The S&P 500 reached
fresh record highs, even as US Treasury yields rose, supported by
stronger cloud earnings and signs of AI monetization. September
proved more challenging as higher oil prices, inflation concerns and
rising bond yields weighed on risk assets, particularly semiconductors.
The Fed also raised its policy rate by 25bp on Sep 16.
Yet, despite these macro headwinds, company guidance continued to

indicate strong AI infrastructure demand, with Micron’s stronger-than-
expected revenue guidance on Sep 30 providing further evidence of
robust demand for memory chips. Memory supply remains tight,
semiconductor capex is accelerating, and the industry’s attention has
increasingly turned to bottlenecks in optical networking and photonics,
as well as concerns around AI regulation and safety. Looking ahead,
tech is likely to remain an important driver of equity returns into Q4,

although elevated valuations and tighter financial conditions leave the
sector sensitive to earnings disappointments. Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, Bloomberg Finance LP. Updated Sep 30. Returns in local currency.
Q3 started with doubts about the AI trade but ended with renewed confidence

1. GLOBAL INDUSTRY BREAKDOWN
Tech hardware, including Lenovo, Advantech, and HP Inc, rebounded in Q3. This was partly driven by accelerating AI

server demand and the ongoing data center build-out, although continuing memory shortages raised costs for
hardware manufacturers. Cloud and server providers, like Oracle, Dell and HPE, also highlighted strong AI-related
demand. Semis and equipment were the weakest performers. Elsewhere, entertainment and telecoms posted modest
declines. Finally, in Q3 investor attention increasingly shifted to AI agents, with Meta’s Muse, Microsoft’s Copilot and

Salesforce’s Agentforce among the prominent offerings.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Bloomberg Finance LP. Median returns taken. Updated Sep 30.
Total returns for tech sectors over Q3 vs. YTD returns

### Page 5

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  Q3 2026 Tech Performance Review |  October 1, 20264
2. SEMICONDUCTORS
Chip stocks were hurt initially by a reversal of the AI trade and then by rising bond yields. The Philly SOX index fell
sharply in Q3, after its worst monthly performance in July (-21%) since 2008. Concerns about higher oil prices and
rising rates continued to weigh on the sector in Aug and Sep. SK Hynix was among the weakest performers, amid an

earnings disappointment relative to expectations and an unwinding of leveraged positions. Its Nasdaq ADRs began
trading in July. Despite these headwinds, the semis outlook remains supported by AI infrastructure demand. Within
semis, potential long-term beneficiaries include suppliers of AI infrastructure, memory including high-bandwidth
memory (HBM), advanced packaging, photonics and semiconductor equipment.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Bloomberg Finance LP. Updated Sep 30. Returns in local currency.

3.1 MAGNIFICENT 7: Microsoft’s earnings rebound
On July 30, Microsoft rose more than 15%, its strongest daily gain since 2008, following results released the previous

evening. The move added ~$450bn to its market cap, the largest one-day increase on record at the time. For context,
that daily increase is larger than the value of any company in Europe (except ASML).

3.2 MAGNIFICENT 7: Market concentration
According to our equity strategists, from end-July to mid-September, tech-exposed stocks within the S&P 500 rose
14% while the remainder declined 3%. This divergence highlights the concentration of market returns in the tech -

exposed sector. In late July, our strategists anticipated a rotation back into technology, which remains underway.

4. REGIONAL BREAKDOWN
After outperforming in Q2, mainland Chinese tech stocks posted large declines this quarter, hurt by July’s sell-off.
The CSI 300 tech series led the declines. Weakness also extended across Asia more broadly, with the KOSPI, Nikkei 225
and Hang Seng Tech down in Q3, driven by a chip sell-off and concerns over further Fed hikes. European tech equities
were more resilient, with the STOXX Europe 600 Tech Index down slightly lower in Q3 but still well above its year-end

level. Within European tech, Infineon underperformed, while Orange illustrated weakness in telecoms outside the index.

to acquire Hugging Face for ~$12.93bn, announced on Sep 3, and AMD’s announced acquisition of World Labs for

$8.2bn on Sep 28.

國際經濟金融週報內容摘要 
美 
國 
美國就業回穩支撐景氣韌性，惟通膨壓力與貿易逆差擴大增添政策不確定性 
經濟 
⚫ 8 月美國非農就業增加 16.2 萬人，優於前月及近一年平均，失業率維持
4.1%，就業市場呈現溫和增長，整體表現仍具韌性。新增就業以餐飲服
務、地方政府教育及製造業為主，惟資訊業仍減少人力。 
⚫ 7 月美國職位空缺數增至 727.1 萬個 ， 職缺率由4.3%升至 4.4%； 惟聘僱
及總離職人數分別減少 27.8 萬人及 26.5 萬人，其中主動離職人數減少
15.7 萬人，裁員與解僱人數減少11.9 萬人。整體來看，企業用人需求尚
屬穩健，但招募及勞工流動動能略有降溫，勞動市場供需仍維持平衡。 
⚫ 8 月美國 ISM 製造業採購經理人指數為 54.6，雖較上月下降 1.0 點，但
已連續八個月維持擴張。新訂單、就業、未完成訂單及進口指數回落，
顯示需求動能有所放緩 ； 惟生產與出口持續擴張 ， 供應商交貨時間延長 ，
原物料價格仍處高檔，整體製造業活動仍維持擴張態勢。 
⚫ 8 月美國 ISM 服務業採購經理人指數由 54.1 升至 55.4，連續第 26 個月
維持擴張。商業活動與新訂單指數分別升至 61.7 及 60.9，反映服務業
需求仍具支撐；惟雇傭指數連續兩個月低於 50，企業聘僱態度仍較審
慎。此外，價格指數升至 72.6，創 2022 年 8 月以來新高，通膨與成本
壓力依然偏高。 
⚫ 7 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差擴大至 886 億美元，較上月增加 24.4%，
主因出口減少 2.1%、進口增加 2.8%。出口下滑主要受原油及非貨幣黃
金等工業原料出口減少影響；進口則受電腦、半導體等資本財需求增加
帶動。累計前 7 月貿易逆差較去年同期縮減 29.6%，顯示美國對外貿易
失衡情況明顯改善。 
重要 
事件 
⚫ 美國於 8 月 30 日以伊朗革命衛隊疑似準備在荷姆茲海峽部署水雷、威
脅商業航運安全為由，對伊朗拉拉克島軍事設施發動精準打擊。伊朗隨
後向約旦境內美軍基地發射飛彈及無人機報復，區域緊張情勢明顯升
溫。由於荷姆茲海峽為全球重要原油運輸樞紐，市場對能源供應與航運
安全的疑慮加深，帶動國際油價及避險需求走高，並增添全球通膨及金
融市場不確定性。 
短評 ⚫ 8 月非農就業優於預期 ， 失業率維持低檔 ， 勞動市場尚未明顯轉弱 ；7 月

國際經濟金融週報 
兆豐國際商業銀行 徵信處 
115.9.7 
一、 美國—就業回穩支撐景氣韌性，惟通膨壓力與貿易逆差擴大增添政策不確
定性 
2026 年 8 月美國就業市場呈現回穩跡象，非農就業人數較上月增加 16.2 萬
人，不僅高於上月修正後的 2.1 萬人，也明顯優於過去 12 個月平均每月增加 3.1
萬人的水準；失業率則維持於 4.1%，失業人數約 703.1 萬人，顯示勞動市場整體
尚未明顯轉弱。從產業表現來看，新增就業主要集中於餐飲服務業增加 5.9 萬人
及地方政府教育部門增加 4.2 萬人 ， 製造業亦增加1.6 萬人 ， 持續支撐就業成長 ；
醫療保健業增加 1.3 萬人，雖維持擴張，但增幅低於過去一年平均水準。另一方
面 ， 資訊業減少2.3 萬人 ， 為主要拖累來源 ， 反映部分產業仍處於人力調整階段 。
勞動市場結構方面，勞動參與率回升至 61.6%，較前月改善，但仍較今年 1 月低
0.5 個百分點；就業人口比率則上升至 59.1%。薪資與工時方面，民營部門平均每
週工時升至 34.4 小時，平均時薪增至 37.75 美元，月增 0.3%、年增 3.1%，薪資
成長保持溫和。整體而言，8 月就業數據顯示美國勞動市場在前月疲弱後回穩，
且前兩個月數據上修，反映就業市場仍具韌性。強勁就業報告公布後，美債殖利
率走升，市場對聯準會 9 月維持偏緊政策的預期升溫，升息可能性亦有所提高。 
2026 年 7 月美國職位空缺數為 727.1 萬個，較上月增加 8.9 萬個，職缺率由
4.3%升至 4.4%；聘僱人數為 505.4 萬人，較上月減少 27.8 萬人，聘僱率由 3.4%
降至 3.2%；總離職人數為 507.2 萬人，較上月減少 26.5 萬人，離職率亦由 3.4%
降至 3.2%，顯示勞動市場流動性有所降溫。就離職結構觀察，主動離職人數為
305.6 萬人，較上月減少 15.7 萬人；裁員與解僱人數為 166.6 萬人，較上月減少
11.9 萬人 。 產業別方面 ， 職位空缺增加主要集中於耐久財製造業(增加 7.6 萬個)；
聘僱人數則以專業及商業服務業減少 18.8 萬人最為明顯 ； 主動離職人數以住宿及
餐飲服務業減少 8.6 萬人最多。整體而言，7 月美國職缺小幅增加，顯示企業用
人需求仍具支撐，惟聘僱及離職規模同步縮減，反映勞動市場流動性趨緩；加以
主動離職率及裁員率雙雙下降，顯示勞工轉職意願與企業人力調整均趨於審慎，
勞動市場供需大致維持平衡。 
2026 年 8 月美國 ISM 製造業採購經理人指數(Manufacturing PMI)為 54.6，較

上月的 55.6 下降 1.0 點，惟已連續第八個月維持在 50 以上擴張區間，顯示美國
製造業景氣持續擴張。細項觀察，新訂單指數由56.7 降至 53.7，雖連續第八個月
處於擴張區間，但需求動能較前月放緩；生產指數由58.5 微降至 58.3，連續第十
個月擴張，產出活動維持穩健；就業指數由52.8 降至 51.2，仍處擴張區間，反映
企業聘僱需求持續增加。供應商交貨指數由58.9 升至 59.3，供應商交貨時間持續
延長；庫存指數則由 51.2 降至 50.6，企業仍持續累積庫存，但備貨動能較前月趨
緩。此外，價格指數維持71.1 不變，原物料價格仍處相對高檔；新出口訂單指數
由 53.0 微升至 53.2， 連續第二個月維持擴張 ； 惟未完成訂單指數由55.0 降至 51.8，
進口指數亦由 55.7 降至 52.5，顯示部分需求與投入動能有所降溫。整體而言，8
月美國製造業活動雖仍維持擴張，但擴張力道較前月略有減弱，新訂單、就業、
未完成訂單及進口等指標同步回落，反映需求成長動能趨緩；另一方面，供應商
交貨時間持續延長、原物料價格維持高檔，加以中東地緣政治衝突、關稅政策及
供應鏈不確定性仍存，可能持續對製造業活動形成壓力。 
2026 年 8 月美國 ISM 服務業採購經理人指數(Services PMI) 升至 55.4，較上
月 54.1 上升 1.3 個百分點，連續第26 個月維持在 50 以上擴張區間，且高於近12
個月平均值 53.7，顯示美國服務業景氣持續穩健擴張。商業活動指數由59.1 升至
61.7，創 2022 年 11 月以來最高水準；新訂單指數由 57.2 升至 60.9，連續 15 個
月維持擴張，創 2023 年 2 月以來新高，顯示需求動能進一步增強。勞動市場方
面，雇傭指數由 47.4 微升至 47.8，但已連續兩個月處於收縮區間，反映企業聘僱
態度仍偏審慎。價格指數由 70.3 升至 72.6，創 2022 年 8 月以來最高水準，顯示
通膨與成本壓力依舊偏高。供應鏈方面，供應商交期指數由52.8 降至 51.3，雖連
續 21 個月高於 50，但已連續第四個月改善；庫存指數則由 51.4 升至 56.7，顯示
企業備貨意願增強。此外，新出口訂單指數及進口指數均升至 56.3，反映外部需
求與採購活動同步回溫。整體而言，8 月美國服務業景氣擴張力道進一步增強，
商業活動與新訂單表現亮眼，惟就業指數仍低於榮枯線，加上企業持續關注關稅
政策及中東衝突對供應鏈與成本的影響，後續仍須觀察通膨與勞動市場發展。 
2026 年 7 月美國商品及服務貿易逆差擴大至 886 億美元 ， 較上月增加174 億
美元，增幅 24.4%。當月出口 3,107 億美元，較上月減少 66 億美元(-2.1%)；進口
3,993 億美元，較上月增加 108 億美元(+2.8%)。其中，商品貿易逆差擴大至1,196
億美元，較上月增加 17.3%；服務貿易順差則增至 310 億美元，增幅 0.7%。出口

下滑主要受工業用品及原材料出口減少 87 億美元影響，其中原油及非貨幣黃金
出口降幅較為明顯 ； 進口成長則主要來自資本財進口增加144 億美元 ， 尤以電腦 、
電腦周邊設備及半導體進口增加最為突出。累計 2026 年 1 至 7 月，美國商品及
服務貿易逆差為 4,472 億美元 ， 較2025 年同期縮減 29.6%； 同期出口成長12.0%，
進口則增加 1.9%，顯示美國對外貿易失衡情況較去年同期明顯改善。 
8 月 30 日美軍以伊朗革命衛隊(IRGC)疑似準備於荷姆茲海峽部署水雷 、 威脅
商業航運安全為由，對伊朗拉拉克島(Larak Island) 火箭發射設施發動精準打擊，
為美方逾一個月來首度對伊朗本土目標採取軍事行動。伊朗隨後以飛彈及無人機
攻擊約旦與阿聯酋境內美軍相關設施作為回應，顯示衝突已由雙邊軍事對抗逐步
向區域層面擴散。至 9 月 5 日美國中央司令部(CENTCOM)表示，因伊朗革命衛
隊向兩艘美國海軍艦艇發射彈道飛彈，美軍已對 3 艘伊朗油輪發動攻擊，其中一
艘位於伊朗重要石油出口樞紐哈爾克島(Kharg Island)外海。美方稱未造成人員傷
亡，惟伊朗半官方媒體指出，哈爾克島錨地附近油輪遭 4 枚美軍飛彈擊中，船員
已展開撤離。由於哈爾克島為伊朗最重要的原油出口據點，戰前約九成原油出口
均經由該地，加上荷姆茲海峽為全球關鍵能源運輸通道，本次衝突已明顯升高市
場對油輪航行安全、原油供應穩定性及運輸成本上揚的憂慮，帶動布蘭特原油期
貨於 9 月 4 日收報每桶 96.28 美元，創 7 月 24 日以來新高。整體而言，美伊衝突
已由原先針對軍事設施的交鋒，進一步延伸至能源運輸與油輪目標，若後續報復
與反制行動持續升級，恐加劇中東原油供應中斷風險，推升全球能源價格與通膨
壓力，並提高市場對美國經濟前景及聯準會貨幣政策走向的不確定性。 
二、歐元區—通膨壓力回升，核心通膨走向值得關注 
 歐元區 7 月失業率持平 6.4%，失業人數持平上月水準。其中德國失業人數增
加 1.1 萬人，失業率持平為 4%；法國、義大利失業人數皆下降 1 萬人，失業率分
別持平為 8.3%、5.8%； 西班牙失業人數持平上月 ， 惟因部分勞動力退出勞動市場 ，
使失業率略降為 10.1%。整體而言，失業率維持在 6.3%-6.4%的低點區間波動，
且 8 月 PMI 的就業指數也有所增溫， 顯示勞動市場的韌性可望延續， 並支持消費
需求表現。 
 歐元區 8 月 HICP 通膨上升 0.4 個百分點至 3.3%，其中因燃料價格上揚，能
源通膨上升 4 個百分點至 14.3%，食品通膨則穩定於 1.2%，迄今能源及化肥價格

### Page 8

本報告係根據研究資料及各項可靠數據作成，兆豐銀行保留報告內容所有權利，未經本行許可，請勿引
用相關內容。

7

上漲仍未對食品價格造成明顯影響 ， 惟不排除存在滯後效應 。 核心通膨(不含食品
及能源)則下降 0.1 個百分點至 2.4%， 主要是服務業通膨下降0.3 個百分點至 3%，
反映旅遊相關通膨放緩；非能源工業產品通膨則上升 0.3 個百分點至 1.2%，主因
天氣炎熱，使消暑產品價格上漲，加上 3C 產品漲價的趨勢影響。整體而言，能
源價格雖然強勁回升，但其影響尚未傳導至核心物價，且薪資上漲壓力降溫，減
輕服務通膨上漲壓力 。 不過 ，8 月以來布蘭特原油大致位於每桶 85-95 美元區間 ，
預期年底前仍將對通膨帶來額外壓力，後續須關注能源漲價可能會透過運輸、物
流等成本管道傳導至核心商品及服務價格，進而提高核心通膨黏性。 
三、日本—製造業產出穩健成長，消費動能仍具韌性 
7 月零售月增 2.4%，部份反映 6 月受梅雨及豪雨天氣影響後的回升，顯示消
費動能仍具韌性。其中耐久財消費持續受到政策及汰舊換新需求支撐，家電銷售
在 6 月大幅下滑後明顯回升 ， 主要受空調等產品需求帶動 ； 汽車銷售額大致持平，
顯示政府自 4 月推動的稅務優惠措施效果尚未消退。另，非耐久財消費亦呈現全
面改善，服裝銷售受炎熱天氣帶動而大幅回升，一般商品銷售則反映國內消費及
觀光需求同步增強，食品銷售亦維持成長， 惟部分增幅可能並非實際需求增加，
若未來食品通膨進一步升溫，恐壓縮家庭實質購買力及消費信心，進而對後續非
耐久財支出及消費動能形成壓力。 
7 月工業生產月增 0.1%，主要係人工智慧相關需求帶動，科技與非科技產業
產出同步增加，其中半導體製造設備、電子零組件及化學工業表現尤為亮眼。出
貨月增 2.2%，高於生產增幅，而庫存僅增 0.5%、庫存率下降 1.7%，顯示產品去
化情況良好，整體供需維持穩健。展望未來，企業預估 8 月工業生產將大幅成長
6.4%，雖 9 月可能略為回落，但PMI 等前瞻指標仍顯示製造業活動持續擴張，預
計第三季製造業產出可望維持穩健成長。 
四、中國—8 月景氣略為回穩，但前景仍有較多不利因素 
8 月官方製造業 PMI 回升 0.6 點至 49.8，連續兩個月處於微幅衰退區間，新
訂單、出口訂單指數雖分別回升至 50.6、50.1，並帶動生產指數回到 50.4，不過，
庫存指數下降 、 就業指數加速衰退 、 企業展望減弱 ， 皆拖累PMI 指數水準 。 此外 ，
供應鏈交期指數上升至 50.1、投入物價指數回升至 56.6，反映交期拉長及成本加
速上漲，使業者營運承壓；產出物價指數回升 2.6 點至 50.4，售價調整速度依然

較為遲緩，不利於獲利表現。另外，RatingDog 的 8 月製造業 PMI 則回升 0.6 點
至 50.9，出口訂單指數為近 6 個月最高，表現較官方製造業 PMI 為佳，反映
RatingDog 調查對象多為出口導向型企業所致。 
官方非製造業 PMI 持平 49，其中建築業活動指數降 0.1 點至 46.9，為連續八
個月處於衰退區間；服務業商務活動指數持平 49.3，非製造業的從業人員指數為
45.4，顯示就業情況仍顯嚴峻，亦較不利於內需表現。另方面，RatingDog 服務業
PMI 增加 1 點至 51.4，較 7 月的低點溫和反彈。相較於官方非製造業 PMI，
RatingDog 服務業因不包含大型不動產開發商而表現較佳，且內需動能有回升跡
象，不過，RatingDog 的產出價格指數跌破 50，為今年首見，反映下游競爭激烈
導致成本難以轉嫁，故雖通膨可望維持低檔，但企業獲利將持續承壓。 
展望未來，G20 峰會將中國龐大貿易順差視為加劇全球經濟失衡的重要因素
之一，國際社會要求中國擴大內需及調整經濟結構的壓力持續升高。此外，中國
仍面臨美國 301 關稅調查、歐盟擴大貿易保護措施等，出口環境仍具不確定性。
未來將關注 9 月 24 日川習會，預計雙方將就關稅措施、出口管制、AI 及先進科
技管制等議題進行協商，若會談結果正向，將有助緩解中美貿易摩擦，降低出口
面臨之政策風險。 
五、印度及東南亞—印度經濟保持強勁、馬來西亞央行利率不變 
印度: 2026 年第二季(2026/27 財年第一季)經濟成長率 （GDP） 年增7.8%，持平於
上季，仍為二十國集團（G20）成長最快國家，成長最快行業依序為:金融服務業
12.1%、製造業 9.2%、公用事業 8.9%、貿易/旅館/運輸/通訊業 8.5%；不過，農業
產出僅增長 3.6%，採礦及採石業則收縮 2.4%。儘管上半年經濟成長表現強勁，
製造業、消費和信貸均展現韌性；不過，油價上漲、印度盧比承壓以及季風降雨
不足等風險持續積累 ， 並透過貿易逆差 、 通膨和企業成本等管道向實體經濟傳導 ，
為經濟前景增添不確定性。 
東南亞:8 月 S&P Global 東協 （ASEAN） 製造業採購經理人指數(PMI)較上月小幅
回落 0.5 點至 52.3，仍保持穩健擴張態勢，其中，新訂單以接近歷史高點的速度
增加，生產活動雖較前月放緩，仍保持穩健；採購活動持續擴張，但聘僱與原物
料庫存小幅下滑，顯示廠商在人力與庫存建置仍偏謹慎，未完成訂單持平前月，
顯示產能壓力依然存在。價格方面，投入成本與產品售價均降至六個月低點，通

膨壓力進一步緩和 ， 供應商交貨時間延遲情況較前月減輕 ； 企業信心進一步增強 ，
未來一年產出預期指數達近四年高點。按個別國家來看，8 月除印尼(49.8)自擴張
轉為收縮外 ， 馬來西亞(50.2)、 泰國(53.8)較上月下滑 ， 新加坡(59.4)、 菲律賓(54.9)、
越南(53.3)均加速擴張。整體而言，在需求穩健、供應鏈改善及價格壓力減輕下，
東協國家製造業可望保持穩健擴張態勢，惟美伊衝突的不確定性仍存，持續推升
通膨預期並干擾供應鏈，同時牽動全球資金流動，仍須留意其對生產及企業獲利
的影響。 
馬來西亞 ：馬來西亞央行 9 月貨幣政策會議 ， 如預期維持基準利率 （OPR）於2.75%
不變。央行指出，儘管全球經濟情勢存在不確定性，得益於強勁的出口、穩健的
就業與內需 ，2026 年上半年經濟成長率(GDP)達 5.7%， 預計2026 年全年可達 5%，
而良好基本面將使 2027 年的經濟成長保持韌性。通膨方面，2026 年前七個月，
平均整體通膨率和核心通膨率分別為 1.8%和 2%，儘管受中東緊張局勢影響，全
球大宗商品價格上漲，但預計物價壓力仍將保持在可控範圍內。整體而言，受益
於全球經濟前景的改善、科技相關出口需求強勁及旅遊業活動增加，為經濟成長
帶來上行潛力，惟地緣政治緊張局勢持續、全球金融環境趨緊以及對金融市場估
值過高的擔憂，仍帶來下行風險。目前的貨幣政策立場與物價持續穩定和經濟可
持續成長的前景相符 ， 未來將密切關注國際局勢變化 、 成本壓力和國內需求狀況 ，
彈性調整政策。

• 升息預期推高公債及信用債殖利率，但升息落地後有趨穩現象：早在聯準會利率會議召開前夕，市場不斷出現
升息聲浪，推高10年期公債利率一度站上5%，壓抑9月以來信用債市表現，尤其以投資級債表現最為弱勢，也
拖累全年總報酬率轉負 ，但利差走勢其實相當平穩 ，８月以來美投資級債利差大致在 75~85基點區間震盪 。
FOMC會議後公債利率略自高檔回落，並沒有因為升息而持續創高，信用債市殖利率也趨於穩定。
• 股市情緒受到影響但未失控 ：9月以來股市情緒受到一定程度影響 ，不過並未出現恐慌情緒 ，僅利率敏感性類
股震盪較大，非核心消費之外的類股年至今仍多收正報酬。聯準會升息落地後，股市單日跌幅也可控。

4
• 風險管理式升息 ，而非典型的升息循環重新啟動 ：9月FOMC會議全票通過升息一碼至 3.75%-4.0%，這是自
2023年7月以來的首次升息。本次會議的重點可歸納如下：(1)利率預估點陣圖顯示至2026年底前還有一碼的升息
空間；(2)上調通膨與經濟成長預估值，同時下調失業率，是一個很鷹派的組合，不過調整幅度其實很小，2026
年的整體PCE通膨和核心PCE通膨僅上調0.1個百分點；(3)判斷本次會議並非典型的升息循環重新啟動，而是「不
承諾路徑的風險管理式升息」。
• 年底前再升息一碼後進入觀察期 ：搭配利率預估點陣圖，以及本行對2027年通膨走勢的判斷(8月的CPI數據已
經看到通膨廣度已經有收斂態勢，搭配本次升息，預期此一趨勢將延續，2Q27通膨有望降至2.5%以下)，本行
基準情境認為年底前再升息一碼後，2027上半年進入按兵不動的觀察期，2027年下半年有望轉向寬鬆立場。

資料來源：左， Fed ，2026/9/17，右，Robin Brooks，2026/9。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/9

推演殖利率4Q26偏高表現、1H27適度回落
市場初步反應: 長率未進一步上揚
美債利率
美債殖利率通常要等確認升息到頂後才會回落
5
• 聯準會升息證明公信力，美債市場大致正向表現：9月FOMC會議前市場已先定價升息，而使得美國10年期公債
殖利率來到5%以上，而在FOMC會議後短率上揚、長率大致穩定，顯示市場主要重新定價近期政策變動，而非同
步大幅上修長期通膨或長期利率風險，是一項相對正面的訊號。
• 推演殖利率4Q26偏高表現、1H27適度回落：根據歷史經驗，美債殖利率通常要等確認升息到頂後才會回落，
4Q26在鷹派論調+期中選舉+高油價的影響下，研判美國10年期公債殖利率維持偏高水位 。2027年隨通膨放
緩，市場確認停止升息後，美債殖利率將適度回落。預估未來兩季美國10年期公債殖利率的區間將處於4.5%-
5.1%，美國30年期公債殖利率則在 5.1%-5.5%之間。尾端風險是「後續的升息不斷超過市場預期 」，不過在
油價不持續失控向上的情況下，尾端風險的機率並不高。

過去一個月企業獲利預估值變化
AI成長趨勢
受惠戰爭
• 景氣擴張週期尚未結束 ，升息對股市影響有限 ：9月FOMC決議升息一碼，但股市反應相對有限 。一方面，市
場已提前消化升息預期；更重要的是，過去一年全球景氣維持穩定擴張，且前景仍偏正向。回顧過往景氣擴張
階段的升息循環，市場往往在升息前因政策不確定性而較為震盪；然而 ，一旦後續經濟數據確認景氣仍處於擴
張軌道，即使估值面受到利率壓抑，企業獲利成長仍有望成為推動股市續漲的主要動能。
• 估值壓縮，選股回到獲利能見度：在股市策略方面，現階段獲利成長動能最強的仍是受惠於 AI長期趨勢的科技
供應鏈。部分科技股已於7至8月率先修正整理，不僅評價壓力有所緩解，也提供較具吸引力的布局機會。不過，
中東地緣衝突尚未落幕，加上國際油價維持高檔，使國防、能源等受惠地緣局勢的族群，成為兼具主題性與避
險特性的配置選擇。整體而言，在聯準會重啟升息的環境下，上半年資金雨露均霑的普漲行情可能逐漸降溫，

但獲利能見度高、基本面持續改善的族群，仍可望獲得資金青睞並展現相對優勢。

©  CTBC
基本面穩健有助降低信用債波動，美元短線路徑轉強
利率走升環境下以非投資級債表現較穩定
市場觀點
資料來源：(左) Fed，2026/9/17；(右) ICE BofA indices，2026/9/16 。中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/9/17
• 基本面穩健態勢不變，有助穩定信用債利差：FOMC會議證實美國經濟韌性，在景氣成長及企業獲利正向循環
未結束前，穩健債信將支持信用債利差走勢持穩，雖然目前美國公債利率已大致反應升息預期，但政策不確定
性仍牽動短線較大波動，利率敏感度較低的非投資級債預期將有相對突出的表現 ，而高利率及高債信品質的族
群則可長期持有以息收為主，而外幣債與美國雙政策連動性較低，新當債與澳幣債走勢可望回歸自身貨幣政策。
• 貨幣政策路徑上修帶動美元走強：美元已從2026年初的降息交易轉向受到美伊戰爭、油價上漲及通膨預期升溫
支撐，但此波強勢更多來自地緣政治與高利率環境 ，而非美國基本面出現結構性轉強 。Fed本次升息後美元指
數當日上升約0.6%，美元指數再度回測100。從聯準會官員利率點陣圖判斷，短期利率路徑較先前預期偏高，

支撐美元表現；惟在通膨回穩後 ，貨幣政策仍有機會重新轉向較寬鬆立場 。留意4Q26共和黨失去絕對多數後
對財政樽節的預期，美元短線路徑轉強。美元指數目標價從97上修至99。

### Page 1

2026 年 10 月 2 日

調査レポート

グラフで見る景気予報 (10 月)

調査部 
【今月の景気判断】 
国内景気は、米国とイランの対立が逆風となっているものの、政府の物価高対策や代替調達の進展などが下
支えとなり、緩やかに持ち直している。内需においては、物価高を背景に家計の節約志向が根強いものの、雇
用・所得環境の改善が下支えとなり、個人消費は緩やかに持ち直している。他方、企業部門では、投資意欲は良
好なものの、設備や建設資材価格の上昇を受けて、設備投資は横ばいとなっている。外需については、日中関
係の悪化によりインバウンド需要の増勢が鈍化しているものの、トランプ関税の影響で落ち込んでいた米国向け
輸出が持ち直していることなどから、全体としては底堅く推移している。こうした動きを受けて、生産にも持ち直し
の動きがみられる。 
今後は、供給制約懸念の後退や良好な雇用・所得環境、堅調な企業業績を背景に、個人消費や設備投資と
いった内需が持ち直すことで、景気は緩やかな持ち直しを続けると見込まれる。世界的な AI 関連需要の堅調さ
に支えられて、輸出が底堅く推移することも下支えとなるだろう。 
ただし、景気の下振れリスクも依然として残っている。米国とイランの対立が長期化する中、原油・石油製品価
格の高止まりや円安進行によるインフレの再加速、原油やナフサといった輸入原材料不足による供給制約の再
燃には注意が必要である。また、日中関係の悪化に伴うインバウンド需要の低迷や戦略物資の輸出規制の強化、
米国の通商政策をめぐる不透明感、金利の予期せぬ上昇による住宅投資や設備投資の抑制、人手不足の深刻
化、地震・天候不順なども、景気の下押し要因として意識される。

【今月の景気予報】

（注）1．3 カ月前～、～3 カ月後は｢現況｣の変化の方向感（改善、横ばい、悪化）を表し、

### Page 5

1. 景気全般 ～緩やかに持ち直している 
2026 年 4～6 月期の実質 GDP 成長率（2 次速報）は前期比＋0.4%（年率換算＋1.4%）と 3 四半期連続のプ
ラス成長となった。イラン情勢の悪化によって物価高の加速や供給制約などに対する懸念が強まったが、政府の
対策が下支えとなり、外需を中心に底堅く推移した。今後も緩やかな回復基調が続くと期待されるが、米・イラン
の対立長期化に伴う原油価格の高止まりや供給制約懸念の再燃による下押しリスクには警戒が必要だろう。

### Page 6

9 月調査の日銀短観における大企業の業況判断 DI は、製造業では、イラン情勢の悪化を受けた供給制約懸
念が和らぐ中、コスト増加分の価格転嫁が進んだことから、石油・石炭製品などを中心に改善した。一方、非製造
業はコスト増加や地震・長雨などが下押し要因となり、宿泊・飲食サービスなどを中心に悪化した。先行きは、米
国とイランの対立長期化リスクが意識され、製造業、非製造業ともに悪化と、慎重な見通しとなった。

日銀短観（業況判断 DI）

（注１）シャドー部分は内閣府による景気後退期 
（注２）2004年、07年、10年、15年、18年、22年、24年、26年の各 3月調査時点で
調査対象企業の見直しが行われたため、前後の系列にギャップがある。 
（出所）日本銀行「全国企業短期経済観測調査」

4～6 月期の企業業績（金融業・保険業を除く）は、売上高が前期比＋2.7%と 4 四半期連続で増加し、利益率
も前期から改善したため、経常利益は同＋11.0%と 4 四半期連続で増加した（業種別の経常利益は、製造業で
は前期比＋12.1%、非製造業では同＋10.2%）。今後も米国とイランの対立長期化による資源価格の高止まりな
どが下押し要因となるものの、国内景気の持ち直しに支えられて底堅く推移するとみられる。

### Page 7

7 月の CI 一致指数は前月差＋1.7 ポイントと 2 カ月連続で上昇した。発表済み 9 系列のうち 5 系列がプラス
に寄与した。中でも投資財出荷指数（除輸送機械）や商業販売額（小売業）のプラス寄与が大きかった。8月のCI
一致指数は鉱工業用生産財出荷指数や耐久消費財出荷指数のマイナス寄与が大きく、3 カ月ぶりに下落すると
見込まれる。基調判断は「改善」が維持されよう。

景気動向指数（CI・DI）

（出所）内閣府「景気動向指数」

8 月の景気ウォッチャー調査の現状判断 DI（季節調整値）は 46.4 と 4 カ月連続で上昇した（前月差＋0.7 ポ
イント）。家計動向関連は、住宅価格や金利の先高観が一部で需要を喚起しているとの声を受けて住宅関連を中
心に改善した。企業動向関連は、好調な観光需要を受けて非製造業を中心に改善した。先行き判断DI（季節調
整値）は 5 カ月連続で上昇しており、イラン情勢が最悪期を脱出しつつあるとの見方がマインドを下支えしている。

景気ウォッチャー調査（季節調整値）

（注）景気の現状判断 DI は、家計動向関連業種、企業動向関連業種、雇用関連業種の景気ウォッチャ
ーによる景気判断から算出される。50 を上回ると「景気が良くなっている」、50 を下回ると「景気が
悪くなっている」という判断が優勢であることを示す。

### Page 8

2. 生産 ～持ち直しの動き 
8 月の鉱工業生産は前月比－1.7%と 2 カ月連続で減少した。生産用機械工業などは増加した一方、自動車
工業や汎用・業務用機械工業などが減少し、全体を押し下げた。製造業の生産活動には持ち直しの動きがみら
れる。製造工業生産予測調査では 9 月は前月比＋3.2%（同調査をもとにした経済産業省の予測は同＋1.6%）、
10 月は同＋3.1%の計画だが、米・イランの対立長期化や相次ぐ自然災害の影響などで下振れる可能性がある。

ビスや小売業などが上昇し、全体を押し上げた。均してみれば、緩やかに上昇している。今後も国内景気の持ち
直しに併せて上昇傾向で推移すると期待されるが、物価高などを受けて下振れるリスクもある。

### Page 10

3. 雇用 ～改善している 
8 月の完全失業率は 2.5%と低水準で推移した。就業者（前月差＋3 万人）の増加は小幅にとどまる一方で失
業者（同＋10 万人）が増加したが、労働市場への新規流入増で労働力人口（同＋11 万人）が増加したことが原
因であり、労働需給のタイトな状態に変化はない。人件費の増加などから中小企業を中心に採用が慎重化してい
る可能性があるが、それでも人手不足感は依然として強く、雇用情勢は良好な状態を維持しよう。

失業率と就業者数

（出所）総務省「労働力調査」

8 月の有効求人倍率は前月と同じ 1.18 倍となった。有効求人数（前月比＋0.7%）、有効求職者数（同＋0.7%）
とも小幅増加した。新規求人倍率は 2.06 倍に低下した。新規求人数（同－1.5%）が減少する一方で、新規求職
申込件数（同＋0.6%）が増加したことで、2021 年 11 月以来の水準まで低下した。人手不足は深刻化しているが、
賃金上昇を背景に企業は新規採用に慎重になっている可能性があり、有効求人倍率は緩やかに低下していこう。

有効求人倍率と新規求人倍率

### Page 11

4. 賃金 ～増加している 
7 月の一人当たり現金給与総額は前年比＋4.3%と堅調な増加が続いている。所定内給与（同＋3.8%）、所定
外給与（同＋4.5%）に加え、夏のボーナスを含む特別給与（同＋5.3 %）も堅調に増加した。また、足元の物価上
昇率鈍化もあり、実質賃金は同＋2.0%と 7 カ月連続でプラスとなった。雇用情勢の良好な状態を背景に今後も
賃金の増加が維持されるうえ、目先は政策効果で物価安定が予想され、実質賃金はプラス圏での推移が続こう。

現金給与総額と所定内・所定外給与

（注）現金給与総額、所定内給与は名目賃金指数から、所定外給与は月間現金給与額から計算  
（出所）厚生労働省「毎月勤労統計」

5. 個人消費 ～緩やかに持ち直している 
7 月の二人以上世帯の実質消費支出は前月比＋0.5%と増加した。また日本の世帯全体の消費動向を表す 7
月の総消費動向指数（実質）は横ばい となった。基調としては、個人消費は緩やかに持ち直している。実質雇用
者報酬は引き続き緩やかな増加を続けているのに加え、消費者態度指数からみた消費者マインドは持ち直して
いることから、個人消費は当面、緩やかな持ち直しが続こう。

実質消費支出（二人以上の世帯）と総消費動向指数（実質）

### Page 12

8 月の商業販売額（小売業）は前年比＋2.7%と 6 カ月連続で増加した。内訳をみると、自動車小売業、各種商
品小売業、飲食料品小売業、医薬品・化粧品小売業などは増加した一方、織物・衣服・身の回り品小売業、機械
器具小売業、燃料小売業は減少した。季節調整値は前月比－1.2%と 2 カ月ぶりに減少した。9 月の新車販売台
数（乗用車、除く軽自動車）は前年比＋3.0%と増加した。

個人消費関連指標

（出所）日本自動車販売協会連合会「自動車国内販売」、経済産業省「商業動態統計」

8 月の消費者態度指数（二人以上の世帯、季節調整値）は前月差＋0.6 ポイントと 4 カ月連続で上昇した。内
訳をみると、「雇用環境」、「収入の増え方」は低下した一方、「暮らし向き」、「耐久消費財の買い時判断」は上昇
が続いた。消費者態度指数からみた消費者マインドは、このところ持ち直しており、良好な雇用・所得環境の下、
今後も持ち直しが続くと期待される。

意識指標で算出される。

6. 住宅投資 ～横ばい 
4～6 月期の住宅投資（実質 GDP ベース、2 次速報）は前期比－0.6%と 3 四半期ぶりに減少した。8 月の住
宅着工戸数は前年比＋6.1%と 5 カ月連続で増加した。季節調整済年率換算値では75.3 万戸（前月比－2.7%）
と 2 カ月連続で減少した。均してみれば横ばいとなっている。今後は金利上昇やイラン情勢悪化を受けた建設資
材価格の上昇などが下押し要因となる一方、所得環境の改善が下支えとなり、横ばい圏で推移すると見込まれる。

住宅着工

（出所）国土交通省「建築着工統計」

7. 設備投資 ～横ばい 
4～6 月期の設備投資（実質 GDP ベース、2 次速報）は前期比－0.9%と 2 四半期連続で減少した。設備投資
に先行する機械受注（船舶・電力を除く民需）は、7 月は前月比－3.7%と 2 カ月ぶりに減少した。うち製造業は前
月比－1.0%、非製造業（船舶・電力を除く）は同－2.6%といずれも減少した。このところ設備投資は横ばいとなっ
ているが、投資意欲は良好であることから、今後は持ち直しへ向かうと期待される。

機械受注と設備投資

8. 公共投資 ～横ばい圏で推移している 
4～6 月期の公共投資（実質 GDP ベース、2 次速報）は、前期比－0.5%と 2 四半期ぶりに減少したが、均して
みれば横ばい圏で推移している。公共投資の先行指標である公共工事請負額は、8 月は地方からが減少し、全
体では前年比－2.4%と 3 カ月ぶりに減少した。工事コストの増加を受けた公共投資デフレーターの上昇によって
実質値の伸びが抑制される状況が続いており、今後も公共投資は横ばい圏で推移する可能性がある。

公共投資と公共工事請負額

    西日本建設業保証「公共工事前払金保証統計」

9. 輸出入・国際収支 ～輸出、輸入ともに横ばい 
8 月の実質輸出は前月比－0.8%と 4 カ月ぶりに減少した。均してみれば、横ばいとなっている。好調な AI 関
連需要が下支えとなる中、イラン情勢悪化の影響が緩和へ向かうことで、持ち直しへ向かうと期待される。また、 8
月の実質輸入は前月比＋1.6%と 4 カ月連続で増加した。均してみれば、横ばいとなっている。当面、中東からの
輸入が伸び悩む一方、代替輸入の増加が見込まれることから、横ばい圏で推移する可能性がある。

10. 物価 ～企業物価は急上昇、消費者物価は緩やかに上昇 
8 月の国内企業物価は前年比＋ 7.6%と、電力・都市ガス・水道などを中心に前年比でプラスが続いている。   
一方、8 月の消費者物価は、「生鮮食品を除く総合」が前年比＋1.7 %、「生鮮食品及びエネルギーを除く総合」
が同＋1.9%と、緩やかな上昇が続いている。政府による種々の物価高対策により当面は安定した推移が見込ま
れるものの、原油価格の上昇が企業のコスト転嫁を通じて物価の上振れにつながる可能性がある。

国内物価

（出所）日本銀行「企業物価指数」、総務省「消費者物価指数月報」

11. 世界景気 ～米国は堅調、欧州も底堅いが、中国は弱い 
世界景気は米国は堅調で、欧州も底堅いが、中国は弱い動きが続く。4～6 月期の実質 GDP 成長率は、米国
は前期比年率＋2.2%に鈍化したが、個人消費、設備投資は底堅く推移し、民間最終需要は約 3 年ぶりの高い
伸びを記録した。欧州は同＋2.6%に加速し 4 年ぶりの高い伸びとなった。一方、中国は前年比＋4.3%に鈍化し
3 年半ぶりの低い伸びにとどまった。先行き、米国は底堅いものの欧州、中国は弱い動きが続く見込みである。

米国・欧州・中国の実質 GDP 成長率

### Page 18

12. 世界の物価 ～原油価格上昇の影響が懸念材料 
世界の消費者物価上昇率は、米国、欧州ではエネルギー価格などを中心に高水準で推移している。米国は 8
月に前年比＋3.4%と高水準で横ばいとなった。ユーロ圏は 8 月に同＋ 3.2%に加速した。 中国も 8 月に同           
＋0.8%と小幅に加速した。先行き、原油価格上昇の影響により、世界の消費者物価上昇率は一段と上昇する見
込みである。

米国・欧州・中国の消費者物価上昇率

（出所）米労働省、欧州連合統計局、中国国家統計局 
13. 原油 ～上昇後、やや下落 
9 月のブレント原油は上昇後、やや下落した。前半は、米・イランの攻撃の応酬が再燃したことを受けて、約 4
カ月ぶりの高値をつけた。後半は、「東西パイプライン」への攻撃などから落ち込んだサウジアラビアからの原油
供給が回復するとの期待などから、やや下落する場面があった。米国とイランとの交渉における駆け引きや、フー
シ派によるサウジアラビアへの攻撃は続いており、原油相場は中東情勢を材料に不安定な推移となろう。

原油価格の推移
